Exported `chainAnchorLocalOffset` + `CHAIN_OUTWARD_OFFSET_M` from the bridge so the scene's rest chains and the live chains use identical geometry — **no C++/WASM change** (the C++ `getAnchors` binding was avoided; `localToWorld` was already bound). `RangeScene`: new `chainMesh` InstancedMesh (2 thin dark-metal cylinders per plate) placed at rest for **every** plate, per-chain rest transforms in `chainRest`; exported `setChainInstance(mesh,index,attach,fixed)` composes a cylinder spanning fixed→attach (reused scratch, no per-frame alloc). `ScopeView` frame loop: each swinging plate's two chains rewritten from `getChains()` each frame; on settle, `chainRest` restored (mirrors the plate snap-back). Engine untouched → no ctest/oracle. **CHAIN-CROSS FIX (owner on device: chains crossed on the way up — all leaned toward centre; small plates crossed near the plate, large near the rack; straightened while swinging then reverted):** the beam-end anchor was nudged inward in X by a fixed `CHAIN_OUTWARD_OFFSET_M` (steel-sim's depth "outward offset" mis-ported onto X), so both chains converged. The DRAWN chain now splays slightly OUTWARD — beam end `CHAIN_SPLAY_FRACTION` (0.5) of the attach offset wider than the plate end, a shallow trapezoid scaled per plate — while the **physics/swing anchor is untouched** (1.5a sign-off preserved). No new automated test (this is pure `useEffect` + display-precision wiring in a component; the underlying `MIL_CLICK_RAD`/`MOA_CLICK_RAD` values and `dialElevationClicks`/`dialWindageClicks` math were already covered in `state.test.ts` since task 1.1). **OWNER (Mac): `npm run typecheck` + `npm test` (unchanged counts) + `npm run build`; then on device — switch to Imp, tap ELEV + a few times, confirm it reads 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00… cleanly (not 0.3, 0.5, 0.8…); switch back to Met and confirm 0.1-mrad clicks are unaffected.** **STOP — owner confirm (typecheck/test/build unaffected by the table-layout tweak, but re-check the DOPE panel and the turret click size/precision fix on device).** **ITER 4 (owner bug report, same session): DOPE panel showed "0.1" windage at wind=0 under Imp while Met showed nothing, at ranges past the 300-yd zero.** Diagnosis: **not a physics bug** — BTK's flight sim does not model spin drift during `sim.simulate()` (confirmed via an existing comment in `engine-bridge/engine-bridge.test.ts`: "No crosswind → windage stays essentially zero (spin drift not modeled here)"), so with `wind.speedMps = 0` the true windage correction is zero and the tiny nonzero value seen is numerical residual from the zeroing solver's iteration (`ZERO_TOLERANCE_M = 1e-5`), not a real effect. The **first fix attempt** (bump the MOA column to 2 decimals, mirroring the ITER 3 turret fix) was tried and then recognized as insufficient before shipping it: unlike the turret's discrete 0.25-MOA clicks, DOPE values are continuous, so a tiny residual like 0.029 mil (correctly "0.0" at 1-decimal MIL) converts to ≈0.10 MOA — still visibly nonzero at 2 decimals, so the two units would still disagree. **Actual fix:** rationalized the existing `hasWind` cutoff, which was an almost-no-op `Math.abs(r.windMilMoa.mil) > 1e-3` (0.001 mil — barely filters any nonzero float). Replaced with a **unit-agnostic negligibility clamp at 0.05 mil** (matching MIL's own natural 1-decimal rounding grid), evaluated once on the raw MIL value and applied to *both* columns before the Met/Imp unit pick: `dropNegligible`/`windNegligible = Math.abs(r.___MilMoa.mil) < 0.05`; when negligible, `drop` renders `"0.0"` and `windHold` renders `"—"` in **either** unit system, so Met and Imp now agree on what counts as "nothing needed" instead of each rounding independently. Reverted the ITER-4-attempt's 2-decimal MOA formatting (both columns back to a flat `fmt(...,1)` — no longer needed once the clamp does the real work; also keeps the DOPE table visually consistent with its own "1 decimal" footnote). **Verified HERE (portable, same sandbox limitation):** Node re-implementation of the row logic swept mil values 0 → 0.1 across the 0.05 boundary — 0, 0.001, 0.01, 0.029 (the exact case that broke the ITER-4-attempt), 0.0499 all clamp to "0.0"/"—" in both units; 0.05 and above show real (and unit-consistent) values; meaningful values (0.5/1.0/3.2 mil) unaffected. No new automated test file (this is display-layer row formatting in a component, same category as ITER 2/3; the underlying `formatDopeRow`/`asMilMoa` math is already covered by `dope-row.test.ts`/`units.test.ts`). **OWNER (Mac): `npm run typecheck` + `npm test` (unchanged counts) + `npm run build`; then on device — Imp, wind=0, open DOPE, confirm ranges past 300 yd show "—" for wind (not "0.1"); switch to Met and confirm it still reads the same "nothing needed"; dial in a real nonzero wind and confirm both units still show a sensible, non-clamped hold.** **STOP — owner confirm.** |

`mirageIntensity(fovDeg, baseFovDeg, base, cap)` — the zoom→warp-strength curve (ported idea from the reference's `zoomFactor = BASE_FOV/fov`), tested for the 1×-zoom identity, monotonic growth on zooming in, and the extreme-zoom cap. **New `scope/Mirage.ts`** (THREE renderer, module-singleton style matching `WindMarkers.ts`/`BulletTrace.ts`): `initMirage(renderer)` builds an offscreen `WebGLRenderTarget` + a fullscreen quad/ortho-camera/`ShaderMaterial` pair; `renderSceneWithMirage(scene, camera, {dt, fovDeg, baseFovDeg, wind})` does the two-pass render each frame (world→offscreen target, then the shader quad warps that texture onto the screen, lazily re-sizing the target if the canvas resized) and advances the drift/noise-clock via the pure model; `disposeMirage()` tears it down. Fragment shader samples the 4D noise at `((uv-0.5)*viewScale − drift)*freq` (crossrange/vertical) + `(−drift.z)*freq` (headwind, churns slowly) + `noiseTime*freq` (keeps evolving even at 0 wind), displacing the UV **vertically only** (mirage refracts light vertically — matches the reference's own rationale), with a defensive UV clamp so extreme zoom can't sample past the render target's edge. `viewScale` (the noise's real-world feature size) is fixed at one `MIRAGE_REFERENCE_DISTANCE_M = 150` m reference depth rather than the reference's 3 depth-varying slabs — a deliberate scope cut, flagged for 1.7d/future polish if a single fixed depth reads oddly at very near/far commits. **D1 resolution (mirage renders in BOTH modes, not gated to Realistic-only):** the plan's own step-2 phrasing said "gate it to Realistic mode," but **D1 explicitly states "Flags and mirage still render [in Steady mode], but they show that steady mean"** — and the owner's own done-when check ("disabling wind (Steady, 0 mph) calms it") only makes sense as a real test if mirage is actually rendering in Steady mode to begin with (otherwise there'd be nothing to observe calming down). Resolved in favor of D1's explicit text: mirage samples the exact same `currentWindAt(...)` helper the flags and the D6 readout already use, so in Steady mode it shows the dialed mean's shimmer (drifting steadily if the mean is nonzero, boiling in place if it's 0) and in Realistic it inherits the field's gustiness for free — zero extra gating logic needed, one line reused. **Wiring** (`ScopeView.tsx`): `initMirage(renderer)` at scene setup (right after `initWindMarkers`); the frame loop's old `renderer.render(scene, camera)` is replaced by `renderSceneWithMirage(scene, camera, {dt, fovDeg: camera.fov, baseFovDeg: SCOPE_BASE_FOV_DEG, wind})`, where `wind` is `currentWindAt` sampled at `(x=0, y=EYE_HEIGHT_M, z=−MIRAGE_REFERENCE_DISTANCE_M)` — the same reference depth the shimmer's visual scale assumes, so the drift you see always matches the wind actually driving it; `disposeMirage()` added to the unmount cleanup alongside `disposeWindMarkers()`. **No new deps, no new assets** (inline GLSL strings only, `three` already a dependency) — precache/offline audit is unaffected. **Perf lever already in place for the owner's on-device check:** `RESOLUTION_SCALE` in `Mirage.ts` (currently 1.0, full device-pixel offscreen target) is called out by name as the first thing to drop if the iPad can't hold frame rate with the extra full-screen pass, per the plan's own step-3 guidance — this sandbox cannot measure real iPad FPS, so that call is explicitly left to the owner's on-device check rather than guessed at. **Verification — real toolchain, same as 1.7b (this session's sandbox has the linux-arm64 native `tsc`/vitest/build binaries installed):** `npx tsc --noEmit` → 0 errors; `npx vitest run` → **23 test files, 213 tests, all passing** (was 203 after 1.7b; +10 here, all in the new `mirage-model.test.ts`); `npm run build` → succeeded, no new precache entries beyond the code bundle itself; `node GameBuild/validation/run.mjs` → **36 golden-vector cases, worst rel diff 0.000e+0 — PASSED** (confirmed via `find … -newer` that no `GameBuild/engine/` source file was touched — D4 holds, this task is pure app-layer). The GLSL shader itself (the one thing that can't be Node-verified) was reviewed by eye against the verbatim-copied, already-proven reference noise function plus standard, well-understood THREE.js render-target/shader-quad APIs (checked against this project's pinned `three@0.185.1` type definitions directly, not assumed) — actual visual correctness is the owner's on-device call. **OWNER (iPad): Scope tab —** confirm a heat-shimmer is visible in the scope image; dial in a crosswind (Realistic mode, a real preset) and confirm the shimmer visibly drifts sideways in the direction the wind is blowing, consistent with the flags/effective-wind readout; zoom in and confirm the shimmer gets noticeably stronger; switch to Steady with the wind slider at 0 mph and confirm the shimmer calms down (stops drifting sideways — a gentle in-place boil is fine, expected, not a bug) rather than freezing solid or staying just as active as before; watch the on-screen FPS (if visible) or general smoothness with the pass running — if it visibly stutters on the actual device, that's the `RESOLUTION_SCALE` knob's job to fix in 1.7d, not a redo of this task. **BUGFIX (owner's first on-device look, screenshot, 2026-07-15):** the shimmer showed as solid green/blue blobs with zero recognizable scene detail — moving and wind-responsive, but nothing legible through it. Root cause: the fragment shader used `intensity` (the dimensionless zoom-driven strength from `mirageIntensity()`, O(0.35–3.0)) directly as the UV-displacement multiplier — `distortedUv = vUv + vec2(0,n) * intensity`. Since UV space only spans [0,1], an offset of order 1–3 doesn't warp the image, it resamples a **totally unrelated part of the source texture** at nearly every pixel, which is exactly what a noise-shaped patchwork of solid colours looks like. The reference avoids this with a **second, separate, much smaller multiplier** (`spatialScale = 0.003`) applied on top of its own per-layer intensity — a two-stage design this port had collapsed into one. Fix: added `SPATIAL_DISTORTION_SCALE = 0.004` (baked into the shader as a literal, matching how the reference bakes its own tuned constants) and changed the displacement to `vec2(0,n) * intensity * 0.004`, giving a real displacement range of ~0.0014 UV units at 1× zoom (≈1 px on a typical viewport — subtle, as mirage should be at low zoom) up to ~0.012 at the intensity cap (≈10 px — a pronounced, visible shimmer at high magnification). `game/mirage-model.ts`'s `mirageIntensity()` itself needed no change (its curve shape/monotonicity/cap were never the bug, only what the shader did with the result) — no test changes. Re-verified after the fix: `tsc` 0 errors, `vitest run` 23/213 still green, `build` succeeds. **This is a shader-only, JS-logic-unaffected fix** (a template-literal snag was caught and fixed in the same pass — backticks inside a shader-string comment had prematurely closed the enclosing JS template literal; removed them). **Visual correctness of the new multiplier is still the owner's on-device call** (this sandbox has no WebGL to confirm the fix actually looks right, only that the math is no longer catastrophically oversized) — please re-check the scope tab. **ITER 2 (owner's second on-device look, screenshot, 2026-07-15): "much closer but still far too strong"** — a distinctly smeared/discoloured band along the horizon. Rather than guess a third arbitrary number from a sandbox with no WebGL to render-check against, **swapped the invented constants for the reference's own real, presumably-already-eyeballed tuning**: `MIRAGE_BASE_INTENSITY` 0.35 → **0.025** and `MIRAGE_INTENSITY_CAP` 3.0 → **2.0** (`game/mirage-model.ts`, both exactly the reference's `BASE_INTENSITY`/`ZOOM_INTENSITY_CAP`), and `SPATIAL_DISTORTION_SCALE` 0.004 → **0.003** (`scope/Mirage.ts`, exactly the reference's own value). By the numbers this is roughly a **14× reduction at 1× zoom** (product of base×spatialScale: 0.35×0.004=0.0014 → 0.025×0.003=0.000075) and a **4× reduction at the intensity cap** (3.0×0.004=0.012 → 2.0×0.003=0.006) — proportionally softening low-zoom more than high-zoom, which fits a screenshot taken with several racks visible at once (i.e. a wide, low-magnification view, where the previous base intensity was doing the most damage). No test changes needed — `mirage-model.test.ts`'s cases pass explicit `baseIntensity`/`cap` arguments to test the curve's *shape* (1×-zoom identity, monotonic growth, cap behavior) independent of whatever the current tuned defaults are, so they were never coupled to the specific numbers being retuned. Re-verified after this pass: `tsc` 0 errors, `vitest run` 23/23 files still green (213 tests, unchanged), `build` succeeds. **This is now a real, borrowed-from-a-shipped-implementation starting point rather than a guess** — still flagged for the owner's on-device eye, since a single-layer port sampled at one reference distance may read subtly differently in character even at a numerically-matched amplitude, and the plan's own 1.7d step explicitly reserves "mirage strength" as an on-device tuning knob, not something a screenshot round-trip should be expected to nail blind. **ITER 3 (owner's third on-device look, 2026-07-15): "Getting better. But the direction is 100% in the direction of the wind... it should primarily move up and at an angle during wind... also, opacity should come down, it's like I'm wearing sunglasses now."** Two distinct, unrelated bugs, both fixed:
  1. **Direction (physics):** `MIRAGE_HEAT_RISE_MPS` (`game/mirage-model.ts`) was **0.06 m/s — ~7.5× slower than even a 1 mph breeze (0.447 m/s)** — so the wind-driven horizontal drift term completely dominated the constant vertical heat-rise term at any wind speed above a whisper, making the shimmer scroll almost purely sideways instead of rising with a wind-driven lean. Real mirage is convective (rises), with wind only leaning that rise at an angle — that's the actual "read the mirage" technique (near-vertical boil at calm, progressively leaning/"running" as wind picks up). Fixed by raising `MIRAGE_HEAT_RISE_MPS` to **2.0 m/s**, chosen so the lean-from-vertical angle (`atan(windSpeed / riseSpeed)`) is ≈13° at 1 mph (reads as "mostly rising, slight lean" — matching the report), ≈66° at 10 mph, ≈77° at the 20 mph slider ceiling (properly "running" at real wind speeds) — no shader change needed, this is purely the drift-accumulation constant in the pure model; existing tests import the constant rather than hardcoding its value, so `mirage-model.test.ts`'s "dead calm only advances y" case stayed correct with zero edits.
  2. **Opacity/darkening (color-space bug, unrelated to the physics fix):** confirmed by reading three.js's own `WebGLRenderer.js` (line ~2342) that a `ShaderMaterial` rendered while `renderer.setRenderTarget(mirage.target)` is active compiles under `ColorManagement.workingColorSpace` (linear) rather than `renderer.outputColorSpace` (sRGB) — i.e. **pass 1 (world → offscreen target) leaves `tDiffuse` holding LINEAR-space colour**, and built-in materials only get the sRGB encode applied via the `colorspace_fragment` chunk when rendering straight to the canvas, which a bespoke `ShaderMaterial` like the mirage quad never receives automatically. So pass 2 was writing raw linear values directly to the screen framebuffer, which the browser then displays as if they were already sRGB — every pixel reads too dark/desaturated, exactly "like wearing sunglasses." Fixed by adding a `linearToSRGB()` function to the fragment shader — copied verbatim from three's own `sRGBTransferOETF` (`ShaderChunk/colorspace_pars_fragment.glsl.js`, read directly rather than hand-derived) — applied to the final sampled colour before `gl_FragColor`. Re-verified after both fixes: `tsc` 0 errors, `vitest run` 23/23 files green (213 tests, unchanged — neither fix touched pure-model test expectations), `build` succeeds. Both root causes were confirmed by reading source (three's actual `WebGLRenderer.js` render-target color-space branch, and the real wind-speed-vs-heat-rise ratio) rather than guessed from another screenshot — the direction fix in particular should be structurally correct now (it's ordinary physics/ratio math, not a rendering nuance this sandbox can't check), though the exact "feels right" lean angle and shimmer strength remain the owner's on-device call per 1.7d. **STOP — owner confirm before 1.7d (integration, tuning, exit).** |

It's adding a bubbling to the view but it's really not discernable if there's a direction to it at all. It's just more frantic at higher wind speed but beyond that, no feel that it's moving up or sideways. May circle back to it later."** Added a proper on/off toggle rather than just parking the feature in code: new `settings.mirageEnabled: boolean` (store-only, like `traceEnabled`/`windMarkerStyle` — not in the save schema), **defaulting OFF**, `setMirageEnabled` action, and a "mirage: on/off" HUD button in the same wind-controls block as the marker-style picker (`ScopeView.tsx`). | — | BUGFIX (pre-existing) | 2026-07-16 | 4538dbc | **Scope reticle not drawn on first load until a Met/Imp toggle** (owner surfaced during 1.8a on-device check). Root cause: `resize()` assigns `reticleCanvas.width`, which clears the 2D canvas + resets its context; the initial `ResizeObserver` callback fires once asynchronously AFTER the first frame drew + cached the reticle, wiping the crosshair, while the unchanged cache key (`zoom\|size\|unit`) blocked the redraw — toggling units changed the key, which is why that "fixed" it. Fix: reset `lastReticleKey = ''` inside `resize()` (declaration moved above `resize` to avoid the TDZ from the synchronous first call) so `drawReticle` repaints the just-cleared canvas next frame. typecheck clean, build green. **OWNER CONFIRMED 2026-07-16: crosshair now shows immediately.** |

When the zeroed target ≠ the cartridge default, the pre-Confirm solve carried a real come-up term at the target, so the player dialed out (bore offset **+** default-zero come-up); Confirm then re-zeroed the solve AT the target, `required` collapsed to ~0, and the baked-in come-up became over-correction → shots flipped high (the "working" rifle's default matched its target). **Fix: the sight-in solve now zeros at the ENGAGED TARGET's distance** (`zeroRangeM: distanceM`, gear + fallback), so `required`≈0 at the target and the only thing dialed out is the bore offset — identical before/after Confirm → POA=POI holds. (Range-A come-up stays a separate concern and still uses the stored `zeroRangeM` in 2.3e.) Removed the now-unused `recommendedZeroM` import. Re-verified: tsc clean, 305 tests, build green. **OWNER RE-CHECK (iPad):** the problem rifle — zero on any target (50/100/200) → after Confirm the next group stays centred (no high/low flip); a fresh rifle's initial miss is now ≤ ~0.9 mil (pure bore offset), not compounded by a zero-distance mismatch. **RE-CONFIRM COMPOSE BUG (owner 2026-07-19: shots perfect while dialing, immediately after Confirm off by ~0.6 mil, repeatable):** root cause — Confirm stored `playerZero = current turret` (a REPLACE), but `resolveShot` applies the stored zero as a baseline UNDER the dial (`applied = aim + dial + playerZero`, D6), so after any confirm the turret is dialed RELATIVE to the stored zero. On a rifle that already had a stored zero (an earlier confirm this session, or one persisted from a prior session — the dev-save rifle), the turret only held the touch-up; replacing dropped the old baseline, and the very next shot shifted by exactly the discarded zero ≈ the hidden bore offset (~0.3–0.9 mrad per the plan §5 fit check — the observed 0.6 mil). A first confirm on a truly fresh rifle is mathematically symmetric (`dial=D,pz=0` → `dial=0,pz=D`), which is why the flow ever looked right. **Fix:** new atomic store action **`confirmZero(rifleId, zeroRangeM)`** (`state/store.ts`) — **COMPOSES** `playerZero += current dial`, stamps `zeroRangeM`, and resets the turret to 0/0 in ONE `set()` (no interleaving with a shot or the dial-subscription between separate writes); `confirmZeroRef` in `ScopeView.tsx` now calls it, replacing the `setPlayerZero` + two dial-reset calls (`setPlayerZero` kept as the raw setter for tests/hydration). Unknown rifle id = no-op. **Tests +5:** `state.test.ts` +4 (fresh-rifle confirm stores dial + zeroRangeM + resets turret; rifle with an existing zero COMPOSES the touch-up; re-confirm with no new dial leaves the zero unchanged; unknown id no-op, turret untouched) and `shot.test.ts` +1 (pure D6 invariance — `(dial=d, playerZero=P)` ≡ `(dial=0, playerZero=P+d)`: confirming must not move the group; the regression the bug would have failed). **GATES NOT RUN — session shell broken (same harness proxy fault as the TS-B/C/D sessions: `Proxy URL: http://localhost:null`); all changes reviewed by eye against the existing types.** **OWNER (Mac):** `npm run typecheck` && `npm test` (expect **34 files / 310 tests**: prior 305 + 5) && `npm run build` (precache 19); engine untouched → `node GameBuild/validation/run.mjs` expected 36/36 zero-diff. **OWNER RE-CHECK (iPad):** with the already-zeroed dev rifle — fire a group (centred) → dial a click or two of touch-up → **Confirm** → fire again: the group must STAY centred (no 0.6 mil jump); Confirm again with no new dial → still centred; fresh-rifle full flow (miss → dial → centred → Confirm → centred) still works; the zero persists across relaunch. Commit owner-side with the rest of 2.3d. *(Interim owner Mac gate run surfaced a leftover unused `recommendedZeroM` import in ScopeView from the zero-at-engaged-target fix — removed.)* **ZERO-DISTANCE FIDELITY FIX (owner 2026-07-19: "zeroed at 100 — but 50 and 200 also read as zeroed, 50 nearly bullseye"):** correct observation of an unphysical model — the "zero at the ENGAGED target" fix made every station self-zeroed (`required≈0` everywhere), erasing the sight-height/drop behaviour the 3-station layout exists to teach, and making the stored `zeroRangeM` observable nowhere on the sight-in range. **Owner chose the faithful model** (offered vs leave-as-is): (1) `sightInSolve` now zeros the trajectory at the rifle's **current zero reference** (`ctx.zeroRangeM` = stored `playerZero.zeroRangeM`, else the cartridge default; the no-gear box fallback still zeros at the target — a POA=POI loaner, nothing to confirm) — so a 100-zeroed 6.5 CM reads its REAL hold at the other stations (~0.2 mil low at 50 from the 2-in sight height, ~0.5–0.6 mil low at 200 from drop); (2) **Confirm hands the come-up off to the new trajectory zero:** `confirmZeroRef` re-solves at the old reference and passes `required = requiredCorrectionRad(drop, windage, T)` to the store; `confirmZero(rifleId, zeroRangeM, requiredRad?)` now composes **`pz_new = pz_old + dial − required`** (default {0,0}) — the come-up part of the confirming dial is absorbed by the new `zeroRangeM`, leaving `playerZero` a pure bore-offset corrector, POA=POI at the confirmed distance, true holds elsewhere, and the original over-correction bug stays fixed *by accounting* rather than by avoidance. (`required` includes any dialed wind, so a wind hold at confirm cancels cleanly — slightly forgiving vs reality; the calm-conditions hint stays as pedagogy.) This also makes 2.3e's Range A semantics cleaner (solve at stored `zeroRangeM` + pz-as-bore-offset, same convention). **Tests +1** (`state.test.ts`, → **311**): handoff case — zeroed@100 + 0.6 mil dial − 0.5 mil required → pz +0.1 mil, `zeroRangeM` 182.88, turret reset. **GATES still owner-side (session shell broken).** **OWNER (Mac):** typecheck && `npm test` (expect **34 files / 311 tests**) && build; engine untouched. **OWNER RE-CHECK (iPad):** zero the 6.5 CM at 100 → the 50 target now reads ~0.2 mil LOW and the 200 ~0.5 mil LOW (not bullseye); dial the 200's hold out and Confirm there → readout flips to "zeroed at 200", the 200 group centres, and 50/100 now read their holds relative to a 200 zero; re-confirm touch-ups still don't jump the group.

| 2.1b | DONE — AWAITING OWNER | 2026-07-17 | (pending) | **Hidden-truth derivation model — pure, engine-free, persistence-free.** New `game/hidden-truth.ts`: `deriveRifleTruth(ranges, draws) → RifleTruth { mvOffsetMps, zeroOffsetRad{h,v}, inherentPrecisionRad }` and `deriveLotTruth(ranges, draws) → LotTruth { meanMvShiftMps, mvSdMps, trueBc, bcSdFraction }`. Each field maps its draw via the D3 **bell-curve map** `bellCurveValue(range, draw) = nominal + sd·clamp(probit(draw), −3, +3)` — probit is **Acklam's inverse-normal-CDF rational approximation** (dependency-free per protocol §4; `invNormalCdf(0.5)===0` exactly, so draw 0.5 → nominal exactly; draw is nudged just inside (0,1) so probit stays finite, then the ±3 SD clamp bounds the result — draw 0 → −3 SD, near-1 → +3 SD). Defined the **catalog-ranges interface** here (`FieldRange {nominal, sd}`, `RifleTruthRanges`, `LotTruthRanges`) so 2.2's catalog can satisfy it; the `bcError` draw maps through the `bc` range (nominal = box BC) to `trueBc`. SD-valued fields (mvSd, bcSd) use the identical map — one level of nesting, no special-casing (D3). Documented authoring constraint: keep `nominal ≥ 3·sd` for non-negative fields. **Import discipline (done-when):** imports ONLY the draw-map **types** from `../persistence` (type-only; persistence is not in the forbidden set) — grep-confirmed nothing from engine-bridge/state/scope/range/shell/debug/React. `bellCurveValue` exported for 2.2/tests. **Tests** (`game/hidden-truth.test.ts`, 9): 0.5→nominal exactly; ≈0.8413→+1 SD (`toBeCloseTo`); monotonic; ±3 SD clamp at draw 0 / near-1; determinism (same draws → identical, `toEqual`); different draws → differing; all-0.5 → every field exactly nominal; **every field within ±3 SD across a full draw sweep (incl. the 0 boundary)**; **adding a new draw key does not change existing outputs (D1 stability)**. **Verified HERE (real Mac arm64 toolchain):** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx vitest run` **24 files / 231 tests green** (was 222; +9; the 1.7a wind-field flake passed this run); `npm run build` green (pre-existing WASM-chunk warning; **precache 17, unchanged**). ## BC truing (`Design/archive/bc-truing-plan.md` — closes DOPE-first plan step 5, D15 lever 2)

Written 2026-07-31 after the owner asked for questions on the plan before implementation;
reviewed against the actual codebase (schema.ts, gear-solve.ts, store.ts, DopePanel.tsx —
every "already exists" claim in the plan's §2 checked out) with no blocking questions, then
built straight through T1→T4 in one session (checkpoints only at T1/T2, owner-verification
stops at T3 and T4 per the plan). No engine source touched — `ctest`/`run.mjs` N/A throughout.

| Task | Status | Date | Commit | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | DONE | 2026-07-31 | (pending) | **The BC fitter — `engine-bridge/bc-fit.ts` (new).** Bracketed bisection over the believed solve: evaluates come-up at `bcMin`/`bcMax` (come-up is monotonically decreasing in BC), rejects with `'needs-more-bc'`/`'needs-less-bc'` + achievable bounds when the asserted hold is outside that reachable range, else bisects to a 0.005 mil tolerance (cap 18 iterations, so ≤20 solves worst case — matches the plan's ~120 ms native / ~350 ms on-device estimate). Reuses `game/firing-solution.ts`'s `requiredCorrectionRad` for the come-up sign convention (positive = up), so the fitter and the DOPE table's own row math can never disagree about what a "come-up" means — this wasn't spelled out in the plan and was confirmed by reading `dope-row.ts`/`firing-solution.ts` before coding. Touches no hidden truth (no `game/hidden-truth` import). **Tests (`bc-fit.test.ts`, 6, real WASM):** round-trip (fit recovers a known BC to 1e-3), monotonicity across the band, identity (asserting the box come-up returns the box BC), both rejection reasons with sane achievable bounds, iteration cap. **Verified HERE:** `npx vitest run` **74 files / 1179 tests green** (was 1173; +6); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` — hit a **sandbox-only** `EPERM` unlinking pre-existing files in the mounted `GameBuild/app/dist/` (confirmed unrelated to this change: even a fresh test file in `dist/` couldn't be removed this session); verified the actual bundle builds clean via `vite build --outDir <scratch>` (147 modules, PWA manifest, no errors). **Checkpoint — continued to T2.** |
| T2 | DONE | 2026-07-31 | (pending) | **Store action + D13 source labelling.** `persistence/index.ts` now re-exports `EffectiveSource`/`EffectiveParams` (were schema-internal only). `state/store.ts`: `setLotEffectiveBc(lotId, bc, source, nowIso)` + pure helper `withLotEffectiveBc`, mirroring `withLotEffectiveMv` — writes `effective.bc`/`bcSource`, preserves the MV side byte-identical, defaults a lot with no `effective` yet to `mvSource: 'box'`. The caller (T3) decides `source` per D13. **Tests (`state.test.ts`, +4):** writes bc+source leaving MV untouched; creates `effective` with `mvSource: 'box'` on a lot that had none; no-op for an unknown lot id; survives a `storeToSave`→`saveToInventory` round trip. **Verified HERE:** `npx vitest run` **74 files / 1183 tests green**; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean (same sandbox `dist/` caveat as T1, same scratch-outDir verification). **Checkpoint — continued to T3.** |
| T3 | DONE — OWNER CONFIRMED ON DEVICE | 2026-07-31 | owner pushed | **Update BC button + confirm dialog.** New `scope/bc-update.ts` (pure, DopePanel-adjacent per the plan's test note): `canUpdateBc` (B3 enable predicate), `angleRadToDisplay`/`angleDisplayToRad` (B1/B6 pre-fill, reusing `units/angle.ts`), `plausibleBcBand` (B4, `[0.5×,2.0×]` box BC), `formatBcRejection` (B2 readable reason, following the panel's existing MIL↔m / MOA↔yd pairing). `scope/DopePanel.tsx`: third header button ("Update BC", enabled only with active gear + a committed target, B3) opens an in-panel dialog — pre-filled from `session.scope.elevationRad`, editable, debounced 250 ms (not per-keystroke, per bc-fit's perf note) via a draft-text/committed-rad split + a 0ms-timeout paint gap before the blocking solve; shows both not-zeroed/not-chronoed cautions (reusing the panel's existing flags, never gating, B5); Update writes through `setLotEffectiveBc` with source = `'trued'` if a `ChronoSummary` exists else `'provisional'` (D13). No new wiring needed for the BC chip to reflect it — `DopeBookScreen.tsx`'s existing status-chip already reads `lot.effective.bcSource` reactively. **Tests (`bc-update.test.ts`, 9):** enable predicate, both angle-unit round trips, band math, rejection-message formatting (both unit/distance pairings, bounds always low-to-high regardless of reason). **Verified HERE:** `npx vitest run` **75 files / 1192 tests green**; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean (scratch-outDir verification again — 149 modules, +2 for the two new files). **OWNER-VERIFICATION STOP:** owner zeroed a rifle, chronoed a lot, committed an 800 m ELR plate, dialed a deliberately-wrong come-up, pressed Update BC, confirmed the dialog's distance/pre-fill, the table reading the asserted value after Update and the BC chip flipping to `trued`; tried an absurd 40 MIL @ 800 m and confirmed it was refused with a readable reason; confirmed B4/B5 felt right. **Owner committed + pushed; asked to continue straight to T4.** |
| T4 | DONE — AWAITING OWNER | 2026-07-31 | (pending) | **Stale-BC signal + DOPE-first close-out.** `persistence/schema.ts`: `EffectiveParams` gains additive-optional `bcSetAt?: string` (ISO), validated only when present, no schema-version bump (matches the 2.1 D6 / existing additive-optional pattern). `setLotEffectiveBc` gained a required `nowIso` param (mirrors `commitChronoString`'s explicit-timestamp pattern rather than an internal `Date.now()`, for determinism) and now stamps `bcSetAt`; the T3 `DopePanel.tsx` call site passes `new Date().toISOString()`. New `game/chrono.ts` export `isBcStaleVsChrono(bcSetAtIso, chrono)` — true only when a `ChronoSummary.updatedAtIso` postdates `bcSetAt`; absent `bcSetAt` never warns (unknown, not stale). Wired as a "⚠ chrono is newer than your BC — re-true at distance" chip in both `scope/DopePanel.tsx`'s status-note line and `shell/DopeBookScreen.tsx`'s chip row — purely informational, nothing recomputed or invalidated (D15: last write wins). **Docs closed out:** `Plans/dope-first-plan.md` (status → COMPLETE, step 5 marked DONE, its two "open decisions" resolved by what shipped, not deferred), `feature-catalog.md` §"Solver truing" (both levers now flagged built, with the scope fence — asserted-hold fit, not a confirmed node) and §"manual truing" (resolved: subsumed by the Update BC dialog's editable field, no separate manual-nudge UI). **Tests:** `state.test.ts` +1 (`bcSetAt` stamped; the round-trip test extended to assert it survives save/load); `chrono.test.ts` +4 (`isBcStaleVsChrono`: stale, not-stale in both directions, unknown `bcSetAt` never warns, no chrono never warns). **Verified HERE:** `npx vitest run` **75 files / 1197 tests green** (was 1192; +5); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean (scratch-outDir verification, same sandbox `dist/` caveat as T1–T3, unrelated to source). **OWNER-VERIFICATION STOP (final):** true a BC, then chrono the same lot, confirm the re-true chip appears in both the in-scope panel and the DOPE book. This plan CODE-COMPLETES `dope-first-plan.md` (all five steps). |

## Wind system — BTK flag/sock + mirage port (`Design/Plans/wind-system-btk-port-plan.md`)

Origin: two complaints — the flag/sock markers are flat unlit primitives where BTK
ships a proper lit shader flag + rigid-body sock, and the single-slab mirage built in
1.7c/1.7d never read as directional and was turned off. Plan locks D1–D9 (2026-07-31),
reads BTK's `WindFlag.js`/`WindSock.js`/`mirage.js`/`scope.js`/`btk.js` verbatim, and
splits into W1–W7 with checkpoints (self-verified, keep going) and owner-verification
stops (declared per task) per protocol §2b. No engine source touched anywhere in this
plan — `ctest`/`run.mjs` are N/A for every task, recorded each time rather than skipped.

| Task | Status | Date | Commit | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W1 | DONE | 2026-07-31 | (pending — rolls into W2) | **Marker sets move onto the range definition; ELR ladder; ground height.** Fixes the live P6 bug: the ELR range used to inherit `RANGE_A_GROUND.laneLengthM` for its marker-lane filter (no `elr-range` branch existed) and planted markers at `groundYM: 0`, i.e. buried up to the flag in the rising hillside. `wind-markers-config.ts`: `WindMarkerSpec` gains `groundYM`; `WIND_MARKERS` split into `RANGE_A_WIND_MARKERS` (unchanged 100–500 yd, `groundYM: 0`) and new `ELR_WIND_MARKERS` (250/500/750/1000/1500/2000 m — 6 of the high line's 8 stations, `groundYM = elrGroundY(distanceM)`); `windMarkersFor(id)` resolves either or `[]`. `ranges.ts`: `windMarkers: boolean` → `windMarkers: WindMarkerSetId | null` (`'range-a' | 'elr' | null`); all four range rows updated. `ScopeView.tsx`: deleted the `laneLenM` filter and its fall-through entirely (P6) — `initWindMarkers` now just calls `windMarkersFor(rangeDefinition.windMarkers)`; Commit message below is ready to paste once confirmed. **UPDATE (2026-07-31, same stop):** owner's first on-device pass reported two related symptoms: "the rise/speed of the mirage needs to be about .55 of what it currently is," "the wind looks... much worse than it is," and "the mirage becomes so fast at about 12 MPH that it all but disappears." Root cause: `advanceLayer`'s drift accumulation (both the wind-driven term and the constant heat-rise term) was running too fast per real mph/second — fast enough that (a) the pattern visibly outran the dialed wind, reading as stronger than it was, and (b) past ~12 mph the drift moved through more than one noise wavelength per frame, aliasing the shimmer into flicker that read as disappearing rather than a real heat-boil slowing/thinning. **Fix:** new `game/mirage-model.ts` export `MIRAGE_DRIFT_RATE_SCALE = 0.55` (this game's own tuned value, explicitly NOT a BTK constant — BTK's own default is what read wrong here), a 6th `advanceLayer` parameter (defaults to the constant) multiplying the WHOLE per-frame drift delta — heat-rise and wind terms together, matching the owner's "rise/speed" framing as one dial — while leaving `MIRAGE_MPH_TO_YARDS_PER_SEC` (P13's exact unit conversion) untouched as a conversion, not a tuning knob. **Tests:** the existing P13 exact-conversion test now passes `driftRateScale=1` explicitly to keep isolating the pure unit-conversion math; the dead-calm and headwind tests' expected values gained the `* MIRAGE_DRIFT_RATE_SCALE` factor (they exercise the default parameter path); two new tests added — one pins the constant at 0.55, one confirms `driftRateScale` scales the wind-driven AND heat-rise terms proportionally together (not just one of them). **Verified:** `npx vitest run` **76 files / 1265 tests green** (was 1263; +2); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` verified via `vite build --outDir <scratch>` — 150 modules, PWA precache **33 entries / 1570.03 KiB** (+0.01 KiB, expected — one new exported constant). `ctest`/`run.mjs`: N/A (no engine touch). Still within the same W6 owner-verification stop — re-check the four strengths (especially near 12 mph) against this new rate before commit. **ITERATION 2 (2026-07-31, same stop):** 0.55 moved the visibility ceiling to ~12 mph, but the wind slider goes to 20 mph — owner asked to try `MIRAGE_DRIFT_RATE_SCALE = 0.2` next (a further ~0.36× cut from 0.55) to see how much more headroom it buys, with an explicit note that lowering the wind slider's own max is a separate, later, out-of-scope decision if 0.2 still isn't enough. Same mechanism as before — only the constant's value changed (0.55 → 0.2), `advanceLayer`'s 6th-parameter plumbing from the first iteration is untouched. **Tests:** the one test pinning the constant's exact value updated to 0.2; the two tests that read the constant symbolically (dead-calm/headwind default-path drift) needed no changes, since they reference `MIRAGE_DRIFT_RATE_SCALE` rather than a hardcoded number. **Verified:** `npx vitest run` **76 files / 1265 tests green** (unchanged count — only a literal changed, no new tests this iteration); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` verified via `vite build --outDir <scratch>` — 150 modules, PWA precache **33 entries / 1570.03 KiB** (byte-identical to iteration 1 — a numeric literal change doesn't move the bundle). `ctest`/`run.mjs`: N/A (no engine touch). **ITERATION 3 (2026-07-31, same stop) — found the real second cause:** at 0.2 the owner reported the SAME ~12 mph visibility ceiling as at 0.55 (drift speed had clearly slowed, but opacity was "still mostly disappear[ing]"), and asked directly whether opacity fades with wind speed. It does, independently of drift rate: `layerFade` (a separate BTK-verbatim mechanism, `MIRAGE_WIND_FADE_SPEED_MPH`, unrelated to `MIRAGE_DRIFT_RATE_SCALE`/`advanceLayer`) linearly fades each layer's intensity to exactly 0 by 15 mph — so at 12 mph the mirage was already down to 20% opacity (`1-12/15`) regardless of how slow its drift was, and from 15–20 mph (a quarter of the wind slider's own 0–20 range) it rendered at flat zero no matter what `MIRAGE_DRIFT_RATE_SCALE` was set to. **Fix, both constants moved together:** `MIRAGE_WIND_FADE_SPEED_MPH` **15.0 → 25.0** (keeps ~20% opacity even at the slider's 20 mph max, instead of hard zero above 15); `MIRAGE_DRIFT_RATE_SCALE` **0.2 → 0.4** (owner: since 0.2's extra slowdown wasn't the fix, split the difference back up from the first 0.55 → 0.2 cut rather than keep compounding a knob that turned out not to be the (whole) problem). Neither constant is BTK-verbatim anymore — both are now this game's own tuned values, called out as such in their doc comments. **Tests:** the `layerFade` "hits 0 at fade speed" test switched from hardcoded `15`/`30` literals to `MIRAGE_WIND_FADE_SPEED_MPH`/`×2`, so it stays correct under whatever the constant is tuned to next; a new test pins that 20 mph (the slider's max) is now audibly non-zero and matches the fade formula exactly; `packMirageLayerUniforms`'s "faded out" fixture wind speed switched from a hardcoded `15` to `MIRAGE_WIND_FADE_SPEED_MPH` for the same reason; the drift-rate-scale value-pin test updated to 0.4. **Verified:** `npx vitest run` **76 files / 1266 tests green** (was 1265; +1, the new 20-mph-visibility test); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` verified via `vite build --outDir <scratch>` — 150 modules, PWA precache **33 entries / 1570.03 KiB** (byte-identical — numeric literals only). `ctest`/`run.mjs`: N/A (no engine touch). Still the same W6 owner-verification stop — check the full 0–20 mph wind range against both new constants before commit. **ITERATION 4 (2026-07-31, same stop) — ELR-specific elevation-falloff bug found and fixed:** owner reported the mirage strong on Range A but "90% gone" at ELR's 750 m station and invisible at 1000 m. Root cause diagnosed and confirmed by computing the actual sight-line pitch from the high line (eye at 9.7 m) against the ELR range's own convex terrain rise (`groundY(r) = 200·(r/3000)²`, `elr-range-config.ts`): 250 m ≈ −1.7° (looking down), 500 m ≈ −0.36°, but 750 m ≈ **+0.29°**, 1000 m ≈ **+0.78°**, 2000 m ≈ **+2.3°** — all uphill, not into the sky, because ELR's far targets sit meaningfully higher than the firing point on the rising terrain. The P17 elevation-falloff uniforms (`MIRAGE_ELEV_FULL_DEG`/`MIRAGE_ELEV_FALLOFF_DEG`, `scope/Mirage.ts`) were still at BTK's verbatim 0.08°/0.14° — tuned for a flat F-class range where any upward pitch means open sky — so ELR's normal, correct, aimed-at-solid-ground sight angles were being read as "panning off into the sky" and faded almost to nothing by 750 m, matching the reported 90%/100% falloff almost exactly (`exp(-(0.29-0.08)/0.14) ≈ 0.22` at 750 m; `exp(-(0.78-0.08)/0.14) ≈ 0.007` at 1000 m). **Fix:** both raised **0.08° → 3°** and **0.14° → 3°** (comfortably covers ELR's full profile out to 2000 m's ~2.3° while still fading if the sight genuinely pans up above the terrain into open sky) — explicitly a starting point for further iteration, not presented as final. This is the first W6 tuning change in `scope/Mirage.ts` itself rather than `game/mirage-model.ts` (P17's uniforms have no pure-model home — they're WebGL uniform values with no test file, `Mirage.ts` being THREE/WebGL-only). **Verified:** `npx vitest run` **76 files / 1266 tests green** (unchanged — no test file covers these WebGL uniforms); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` verified via `vite build --outDir <scratch>` — 150 modules, PWA precache **33 entries / 1570.02 KiB** (byte-identical, numeric literals only). `ctest`/`run.mjs`: N/A (no engine touch). Still the same W6 owner-verification stop — check ELR's full station ladder (both firing points) against the new elevation constants, alongside the wind-range check from iteration 3, before commit. |
| W7 | DONE | 2026-07-31 | (pending — push) | **Close-out.** `Design/feature-catalog.md`: added a new "Wind-reading renderers — flags/socks + layered heat mirage" entry under §B (Built section) — there was no pre-existing flag/sock/mirage entry to flip (the original Increment-1 ship only mentioned "wind controls/HUD" generically), so this documents the whole W1–W6 rebuild in one place: the BTK-faithful flag/sock port, the layered mirage replacing the old single flat layer, the new strength preset, and the four on-device tuning constants (drift rate, wind-fade ceiling, and the ELR-specific elevation-falloff retune), plus the plan's §7 deferred item (mirage as a readable wind-call instrument) logged as a follow-up rather than dropped. Grepped the whole `GameBuild/` tree for stale single-layer/`MIRAGE_REFERENCE_DISTANCE_M` references (the plan's explicit W7 instruction): the only two hits are the intentional historical file-header comments in `game/mirage-model.ts`/`.test.ts` explaining what was removed and when — nothing stale or broken. **Plan archived** (owner: "close out the plan, archive it") — matching the established convention for completed owner-requested plans (`target-system-plan.md`, `elr-probe-plan.md`, `bc-truing-plan.md`): the plan's own status header rewritten to COMPLETE/ARCHIVED (pointing to the feature-catalog entry and these PROGRESS rows as the authoritative record, not itself), then moved `Design/Plans/wind-system-btk-port-plan.md` → `Design/archive/wind-system-btk-port-plan.md`. **Verified:** no code changed this task (docs only), so the three gates were not re-run; the last code-touching iteration (W6 iteration 4) already confirmed all three green. `ctest`/`run.mjs`: N/A (no engine touch, as for every task in this plan). **Boundary: plan complete.** **Commit: `commit + push`** (this is the plan's final commit point — see the message below, now covering everything since the last owner-facing message: the strength control, all four tuning iterations, and this close-out). |

## Target surface system (owner-requested 2026-07-18 — persistent hit marks + per-plate paint)

Origin: hits leave no lasting mark (the first 1.5c world-anchored "scuff" clipped the
swinging plate and was dropped — see deferred obs (1.5c)). Owner reviewed both fixes and
chose the **BTK texture-paint architecture** over a minimal instanced-splat sprite: the
C++ SteelTarget's per-target paint buffer becomes the plate's actual surface (one
`DataArrayTexture` layer per plate, UV'd disc geometry, `onBeforeCompile` material
patch). Marks then ride the swing by construction (no re-projection, no z-fight), and
per-plate **color / patterns / new shapes / repaint** all become config on the same
system instead of future one-off patches. **Supersedes deferred obs (1.5a)/(1.5c)
"the game draws its own marks — texture path intentionally unused."** Marks are
session-persistent, plates only (misses keep the transient puff). Three tasks, protocol
§3-sized, STOP after each.

| Task | Status | Date | Commit | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TS-A | DONE — AWAITING OWNER | 2026-07-18 | (owner-side) | **Engine paint contracts pinned by native tests — zero C++ source changes needed.** Audit: `setColors` / `clearImpacts` (the repaint hook) / `initializeTexture` / `getTexture` / `getTextureWidth/Height` are all already implemented in `engine/src/rendering/steel_target.cpp` AND bound in `bindings.cpp` — so no engine edit, no oracle risk, no WASM rebuild this task. New **`engine/tests/test_steel_target_paint.cpp`** (7 tests) pins what TS-B/TS-C depend on: buffer = (2·size)×size RGBA (front face left half / back right); default red (255,40,40) fill; `setColors`+`initializeTexture` repaint the whole buffer; **a downrange hit in the game's geometry (normal (0,0,-1) at identity, bullet vel −Z ⇒ vel·normal>0 ⇒ engine "back face") paints the RIGHT half** — the half TS-B's shooter-facing UVs must sample; uprange hit → left half; local (x,y)→texel mapping (u=0.5+x/w compressed into the struck half; offset hit lands at the computed texel); `clearImpacts()` wipes to clean paint. `tests/CMakeLists.txt` gains the file. **Iter 1 (owner ran gates 2026-07-18): 10/12 green, 2 red** (`UprangeHitPaintsLeftHalfOnly`, `OffsetHitMapsLocalXYToTexel`) — diagnosed from `LastTest.log` as a TEST bug, not engine: expected pure metal `[7,8,9]` at the splat center, got `[8,8,9]` (one count off, one channel). The ellipse does write pure metal at blend 0, but a randomized spike segment can graze the center texel via integer truncation at small t with fade = t² > 0, nudging a channel — so exact-equality at the center was only luck-deterministic (test 9 happened to pass). **Fix: center assertions became `texelNear` (±12 counts of metal — spike-graze bound ~fade 0.03, still nowhere near paint red 200); untouched-half checks stay exact** (provably unreachable by the draw loops). Engine behavior confirmed correct throughout — the failures VALIDATED the paint path's real behavior. **OWNER re-ran 2026-07-18: 12/12 green — CONFIRMED ("All passed. Proceed."). TS-A COMPLETE.** Vitest/golden vectors unaffected (no app or engine-source change). Commit owner-side (`target-surface/TS-A: engine paint-contract native tests`). |

Then **iPad**: same loop + memory/fps feel. STOP — owner confirm; TS-C closes the target-surface thread. |
| TS-D | code-complete — AWAITING OWNER (gates + visual) | 2026-07-18 | (owner-side) | **Hanging-plate settle-orientation fix — the front/back-face rework (TS-B/C) surfaced it.** Symptom (owner): a plate that SPINS on an off-centre hit settled **facing away with chains crossed**, then popped around when the engine reported settled; also hit marks showed **"only 1 hit per side"** (a flipped plate registers/reveals the opposite texture half). **Owner decision (2026-07-18): "twist, then untwist to face you"** (keep the off-centre spin flavor, always return to facing the shooter). **This took TWO passes — the first was insufficient; the owner's "still happening, but only on the 300-yd MIDDLE (4″) target; 500-yd works" report was the key clue.** Root cause (confirmed by native sims across the plate ladder): three compounding problems, all worst on LIGHT plates. (1) A bullet strike imparts a **cartoonish 100–440 rad/s spin** on small plates — tiny rotational inertia, and mass floored at `MIN_MASS`. (2) The extracted twist angle used `2·atan2(qy, qw)` which **flips sign on the quaternion's w<0 hemisphere** (reached after a fast multi-rev spin), turning the restoring spring into an attractor that made 180° STABLE. (3) The chains **actively destabilize** the facing-forward twist for light plates (torque ∝ 1/inertia), so a fixed-**acceleration** spring is overpowered on exactly the small racks (6″ and under) — which is why 8″+ worked and the 300-yd 4″/500-yd 6″ didn't. **Fix (C++ physics only, `steel_target.cpp`/`.h`):** (a) **angular-speed cap** `MAX_ANGULAR_SPEED = 12 rad/s` (realistic + tames the violent spin); (b) hemisphere-corrected twist via new `twistAboutY()` helper; (c) **hard twist limit** `MAX_TWIST = 60°` about world Y — chains "bind", so the plate physically **cannot flip or cross the chains** (structural guarantee, not tuning); (d) **torque-form** torsional spring `TWIST_STIFFNESS = 20 N·m/rad` via a factored `applyTorque` helper — a torque (÷inertia) competes with the chain destabilization **uniformly across plate sizes** where the earlier fixed-accel spring failed; (e) **twist-aware settle** — `isMoving()` won't report settled until twist < ~3°, so the snap-to-rest is always invisible and never freezes mid-twist. `applyImpulse` refactored onto `applyTorque` (behavior identical). **No TS change** — the plate now settles facing forward, so `ScopeView.tsx:887`'s snap is a no-op; the "1 hit per side" symptom is a downstream consequence that resolves with it. **Verified (this session):** native **stress sweep of 648 configs** (all 6 plate sizes × 6 offsets × 3 heights × 3 speeds to 800 m/s × {1,3} hits) → **0 settle facing away**; native `engine_tests` **15/15** (incl. new `test_steel_target_settle.cpp`: all-sizes untwist, swing-still-works, 4″ sequential-hits-stay-on-one-half); **WASM rebuilt**; app `steel-target.test.ts`+`engine-bridge.test.ts` **9/9** against it. Settle takes ~9–13 s (spin bleed-off; correct orientation throughout) — tune `TWIST_STIFFNESS`/`MAX_ANGULAR_SPEED`/`MAX_TWIST` if the feel wants adjusting. **IMPORTANT — `build-wasm/` is git-ignored:** to see this on any other checkout/device the owner must rebuild the engine (`cd GameBuild/engine/build-wasm && emmake make -j`), restart `npm run dev`, and on iPad accept the PWA update (SW serves the old embedded bundle until then). **OWNER gates:** `GameBuild/app`: `npm run typecheck` && `npm test` && `npm run build`; oracle vectors unaffected (steel physics isn't an oracle vector). **VISUAL (dev → Scope):** hit the 300-yd middle (4″, orange) plate off-centre — it twists, then drifts back to face you, chains uncrossed, no pop; repeat hits accumulate marks on the visible face. **Iter 2 (owner 2026-07-18): "better, but now barely any twist — even a hard off-centre hit doesn't spin; 89.9° then settling back would be good."** Root of the over-damping: the torque-form spring's natural frequency is ∝1/√inertia, so light plates barely rotated before it snapped them back. **Refined to a properly decoupled twist model:** twist about world-Y is now its OWN DOF — the chains drive only the swing (their world-Y torque is snapshotted and restored each substep, so they no longer fight or destabilize twist), and twist is a clean torsional oscillator with a **fixed-frequency** spring (`TWIST_STIFFNESS = 60` rad/s²·rad⁻¹ ≈ ωₙ 7.7) + dedicated damping (`TWIST_DAMPING = 0.03`, ζ≈0.3) so every plate — light or heavy — spins out to the SAME visible peak (`≈ cap/ωₙ`) then rings back and settles facing forward. `MAX_TWIST` raised to 95° (backstop below a flip). Verified: native stress **648 configs → 0 settle-away, peak true Y-twist 89.2°** (never flips), + new `OffCentreHitProducesVisibleButBoundedTwist` unit test (peak twist 30°–100°); `engine_tests` **16/16**; WASM rebuilt; app bridge tests **9/9**. Feel knobs in `steel_target.h`: `TWIST_STIFFNESS`↑ = smaller/stiffer twist, `TWIST_DAMPING`↓ (toward 0) = more ring, `MAX_ANGULAR_SPEED` sets the peak. STOP — owner confirm. |

## Test Range — environment sandbox (owner-requested plan, `Design/archive/test-range-environment-plan.md`)

Origin: a new 100-yd wooded "Test Range" — a reusable environment module that
doubles as (1) a prototype for upgrading Range A / Zero Range's visual world
(terrain/trees/sky/mountains/clouds) before touching those live ranges, and (2)
a permanent sandbox for future target types. Four stages, protocol-sized, STOP
after each; the plan's own code snippets were followed closely.

Background hills look great, there's just something close that's obscuring the target.")** The owner's report (background hill now fine; a DIFFERENT, close bump sits at the target regardless of tuning) finally pointed at the real bug — not in `test-range-config.ts`'s numbers at all, in **`environment/terrain.ts`**'s mesh-building code, invisible to every prior test because those all exercise `makeTerrainSampler` directly, never the actual rendered `PlaneGeometry`. **Bug:** `buildTerrain` set each vertex's height via `sampler(x, -yLocal)`, but the mesh is then rotated `-Math.PI/2` about X and translated by `position.z = -lengthM/2` — so a vertex's real world z ends up `-yLocal - lengthM/2`, not `-yLocal`. Verified the transform empirically with a throwaway `THREE.PlaneGeometry.rotateX/translate` script. Net effect: the ground rendered at the target's actual world z (−91.44 m) was being *height-sampled* at z≈+159 m — past `zFlatToM+zBlendM` (150 m), i.e. **always fully relief-unlocked**, independent of any hill/mask value — so every prior round's hill retuning (Iters 2-4) could never visibly change what rendered at the target, exactly matching "no discernible change." This also explains Iter 2's original "off to the side" report: with the old x-only mask, the offset made the WRONG stretch of relief show up near the target regardless of where the hill was conceptually placed. **Fix — `environment/terrain.ts` only** (`test-range-config.ts` numbers untouched): height now sampled at `sampler(x, -yLocal - lengthM / 2)`, i.e. the exact world z the vertex will render at. Also split the pure displacement loop out into a new exported `buildLaneGeometry(cfg, sampler)` (no `loadPbrMaterial`/DOM dependency) so it's directly vitest-able — `buildTerrain` now just calls it. **New `environment/terrain.test.ts`** (+3 tests, total 15): builds the real geometry, applies the identical rotate+translate the mesh gets, and asserts the resulting world-space vertex heights match `makeTerrainSampler` exactly at the target, at the hill, and across a spot-check sweep of the whole mesh — this is the regression guard that would have caught Iters 2-4's ineffectiveness immediately. **Verified HERE:** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx vitest run` **336/336 green** (333 + 3 new, no flake this run); `npm run build` clean (same two pre-existing benign warnings).
