STOP — owner check on device required (DevTools Test Range tab first, then in-game) before Stage 4 (mountains + clouds + polish) can be authorized. **OWNER FEEDBACK 2026-07-21 (in-game screenshots, real player flow):** two bugs. (1) Tree canopy floated visibly disconnected above a short buried trunk stump — root cause: `THREE.CylinderGeometry` is centred on its own local origin by default (spans `-height/2..+height/2`), but the placement matrix's `pos` treats local y=0 as ground; the trunk was therefore half-buried with its top far short of the canopy's baked-in tier-1 bottom. Canopy tier offsets (conifer 3.2/4.6/5.9, deciduous 3.8) were tuned assuming a ground-based trunk, so the ONLY fix needed was translating each trunk geometry up by half its own height (`coniferTrunkGeo.translate(0, 1.1, 0)`, `deciduousTrunkGeo.translate(0, 1.3, 0)`) so its base sits at local y=0 — no canopy-offset changes required; trunk top now overlaps the lowest tier/blob by 0.3–0.4 m, hiding the seam. (2) Grass tufts: owner reported "nothing in the view of the target" (screenshot showed the ENTIRE scope view — at any magnification — filled with a wall of indistinct pale-green flat rectangles) and "the green squares are indistinct enough to not know what they're supposed to be." Root cause was two compounding issues: (a) the old `SHOOTER_CLEAR_M = 3` (hardcoded local const in `generateScatterPlacements`) let tufts spawn as close as 3 m from the camera at (0, 1.6, 0) — a tuft that close subtends more angle than the entire sight picture at any real scope magnification, so it (and the dozens of others within a few metres, since `grassTuftCount: 450` over a 32×30 m box is dense) blots out the target 100 yd downrange entirely; (b) the crossed-quad "X" shape (two flat `PlaneGeometry` planes) reads as a flat green card/square up close, not grass. Owner's explicit direction: *"the rules on this can be much more lax than the hills"* — i.e., unlike Stage 2's terrain-flatness fix (which needed exact coordinate-mapping precision), the shooter-clearance rule just needs to be generously loose, no rigor required. **Fix:** promoted the clear radius to a real config field `cfg.cover.shooterClearM` (was a hardcoded const) so it's tuned per-range and covered by a test, set to `18` m for the Test Range (`grassZoneM` raised 30→34 to keep a real band beyond it), `grassTuftCount` cut 450→140 to match the now-smaller usable band (avoids the remaining tufts still reading as a wall); added a pathological-PRNG safety net (`if (Math.hypot(x,z) < SHOOTER_CLEAR_M) z = -SHOOTER_CLEAR_M`, mirroring the trees' `drawClearOfLane` snap) since 50 retry attempts is no longer trivially guaranteed to succeed at the larger radius. Replaced the crossed-quad grass geometry with a 5-blade tapered fan (each blade a single flat triangle — wide base, pointed tip — fanned around a centre point at different radii/heights/angles, dark-base→light-tip vertex-color gradient kept) via a new generalized N-geometry merge helper (`mergeManyGeometriesSimple`, replacing the old 2-geometry-only version) — reads as a grass clump from any angle instead of a flat green card. **Files:** `environment/environment-config.ts` (`cover.shooterClearM` field + docs, safety net), `range/test-range-config.ts` (tuned `cover` values), `environment/trees.ts` (trunk `.translate()` fix ×2), `environment/ground-cover.ts` (blade-fan geometry + N-geometry merge). **New test:** `environment-config.test.ts` — "grass tufts clear the shooter by shooterClearM." **Verified:** typecheck clean; `npx vitest run` **337/337 green** (15/15 in `range/environment/`; the one pre-existing flaky statistical test in `wind-field.test.ts`, unrelated to this work, passed on this run); `npm run build` clean (same two pre-existing benign warnings). STOP — owner re-check on device required (DevTools Test Range tab, then in-game at a few different scope magnifications) before Stage 4 can be authorized. **OWNER FEEDBACK ROUND 3 2026-07-21 (in-game screenshots): "Getting there, still nothing behind the target or in range of the scope, have to scroll around to find anything. Trees look ok but the canopy need to be a much lighter green, they're currently very dark nearly black. The grass sections are much better but too tall, about half current height would be good."** Three fixes. (1) **Nothing behind the target / have to scroll to find anything** — root cause: `drawClearOfLane`'s uniform `minAbsX` (28 m) x-clearance rule applies identically to every tree/bush/rock band regardless of z-distance, so ALL vegetation everywhere sits at least 28 m off the sight line. Worked through the angle math (`atan(x_offset / z_distance)`): for a fixed x-offset, a CLOSER object subtends a LARGER angle off the boresight, so simply moving the existing behind-target band's near edge closer to the target would make centring worse, not better — the real fix has to exempt a band from the x-clearance rule entirely. **Fix — `environment/environment-config.ts`**: extended the internal `Band` type (and the `EnvironmentConfig.trees.bands` array type) with an optional `allowOnLane?: boolean`; `drawClearOfLane` now tracks which band a candidate point was drawn from and accepts immediately (skipping the `minAbsX` check and the pathological-PRNG safety net) when that band is flagged `allowOnLane` — trusted only for bands that sit safely behind the target. **`test-range-config.ts`**: added a 4th tree band, `{ xMin: -40, xMax: 40, zMin: -280, zMax: -140, allowOnLane: true }` — a centred backdrop cluster whose near edge (−140 m) clears the gong (−91.44 m) by ~49 m, well past the existing "≥40 m so misses disappear into the woods" convention, so it can never stand between the shooter and the target. This band is shared with bushes/rocks too (`generateScatterPlacements` draws from the same `cfg.trees.bands`), so a few also land centred behind the target now. **`environment-config.test.ts`**: updated the two lane-clearance assertions (trees; bushes/rocks) to exempt points that fall inside an `allowOnLane` band; added a new test asserting the backdrop band's near edge clears the gong by the buffer and that it actually produces on-lane trees. (2) **Canopy "very dark, nearly black"** — investigated `lighting.ts` (HemisphereLight 0.9, DirectionalLight 1.5) as a candidate cause but chose not to retune scene-wide lighting, since that risks regressing every other already-approved element (terrain, sky, rocks) for a foliage-only complaint; the grass tufts (unlit `MeshBasicMaterial`) already prove vertex colors render faithfully once unlit, so the canopy/bush materials' `MeshStandardMaterial` + `flatShading` combination reading dark on shadow-facing faces is a material/lighting interaction, not a placement bug. **Fix — palette brightening only**: `test-range-config.ts`'s `trees.palette` and `ground-cover.ts`'s `BUSH_PALETTE` both replaced with substantially lighter greens (roughly 1.6–2× each channel) so even shadow-side faces read as visible green rather than crushing to black. (3) **Grass too tall, "about half current height would be good"** — straightforward: `ground-cover.ts`'s `GRASS_TALLEST_BLADE_M` halved (0.42→0.21) and the other four blade heights in `buildGrassTuftGeometry` halved to match (0.34/0.38/0.3/0.36 → 0.17/0.19/0.15/0.18). **Verified HERE:** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx vitest run` **338/338 green** (337 + 1 new backdrop-band test; the previously-noted pre-existing flaky `wind-field.test.ts` statistical test did not flake on this run); `npm run build` clean (same two pre-existing benign warnings). STOP — owner re-check on device required (DevTools Test Range tab, then in-game — confirm something reads as backdrop when centred on the gong, canopy no longer near-black, grass roughly half its previous height) before Stage 4 (mountains + clouds + polish) can be authorized. **OWNER FEEDBACK ROUND 4 2026-07-21 (in-game screenshot): "Much better but the canopy is still way too dark, is it a problem with color or lack of light?" — ROOT CAUSE FOUND.** The palette-brightening fix from round 3 produced ZERO visible change to the canopy (screenshot showed solid black, identical to before) — the tell that a value was being multiplied by zero somewhere, not just under-lit, since even a much lighter albedo would have shown *some* lightening under real light starvation. Root cause: `coniferCanopyMat`/`deciduousCanopyMat` (`trees.ts`) and the bush material (`ground-cover.ts`) all set `vertexColors: true` on a `MeshStandardMaterial`, but their geometries (`buildConiferCanopyGeometry`/`buildDeciduousCanopyGeometry`, a plain `IcosahedronGeometry` for bushes) never attach a per-vertex `color` `BufferAttribute` — the tint was always meant to come entirely from `InstancedMesh.setColorAt` (instance color) instead. `vertexColors: true` makes Three.js's shader also multiply by the geometry's `color` attribute (`USE_COLOR` path); with no such attribute bound, that read comes back as zero, zeroing the whole output regardless of the instance tint or how bright the palette is — `USE_INSTANCING_COLOR` (the actual per-tree/per-bush tint mechanism) is gated independently and would have worked fine on its own. This is why round 3's palette brightening (`test-range-config.ts`'s `trees.palette`, `ground-cover.ts`'s `BUSH_PALETTE`) had no visible effect — those hex values were still being multiplied into `vColor` correctly via `setColorAt`, but then zeroed by the missing-attribute `USE_COLOR` multiply immediately after. Grass tufts were never affected since `buildGrassTuftGeometry` (`ground-cover.ts`) explicitly does set its own `color` attribute (the dark-base/light-tip gradient), so its `vertexColors: true` had a real attribute to read. **Fix — `trees.ts` + `ground-cover.ts` only** (no config/palette changes needed this round): removed `vertexColors: true` from `coniferCanopyMat`, `deciduousCanopyMat`, and the bush material — each now relies solely on `InstancedMesh.setColorAt`/`instanceColor` for its per-instance tint, with no unbound-attribute multiply in the way. Round 3's brightened palettes are kept (still a reasonable "mixed forest" spread; no reason to revert now that the actual zeroing bug is fixed). **Verified HERE:** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx vitest run` **338/338 green** (no test changes — this is a THREE-material/shader bug in the untestable-under-node-env class, same as `trees.ts`/`ground-cover.ts` generally); `npm run build` clean (same two pre-existing benign warnings). STOP — owner re-check on device required (canopy and bushes should now show their actual palette greens, not black) before Stage 4 can be authorized. **OWNER FEEDBACK ROUND 5 2026-07-21 ("Much better. Is it possible to smooth the edges without significant changes in the geometry?")** — the visible hard triangular facets on conifer canopies, deciduous canopy blobs, and bushes all came from an explicit `flatShading: true` on their `MeshStandardMaterial`s, which forces the shader to derive a per-triangle flat normal (via screen-space derivatives) regardless of the geometry's own normal attribute. `ConeGeometry` (conifer tiers) and `IcosahedronGeometry` (deciduous blobs, bushes) both already carry smooth analytic vertex normals from construction (a cone's slant surface, an icosahedron's spherical push-out), so simply dropping `flatShading` restores GPU interpolation across faces — same vertices/triangles/indices, no geometry rebuild. **Fix — `trees.ts` (`coniferCanopyMat`, `deciduousCanopyMat`) + `ground-cover.ts` (bush material)**: removed `flatShading: true` from all three, matching the user's "without significant changes in the geometry" ask exactly (one material-flag removal each). Rocks (`buildJitteredRockGeometry`, PBR material, no `flatShading` flag to begin with) are unaffected and intentionally stay jagged — their irregular look comes from vertex jitter, not flat shading, and reads correctly as a boulder either way. **Verified HERE:** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx vitest run` **338/338 green** (no test changes — pure shading-flag change, same untestable-under-node-env class as the rest of `trees.ts`/`ground-cover.ts`); `npm run build` clean (same two pre-existing benign warnings). STOP — owner re-check on device required (canopy/bush edges should read as smoothly shaded rather than faceted, everything else unchanged) before Stage 4 (mountains + clouds + polish) can be authorized. |
| 4 | DONE — owner confirmed 2026-07-22 | 2026-07-22 | (owner-side) | **Mountains + drifting clouds + polish — owner-authorized ("Let's move on to the next part.") after Stage 3 round 5's smoothing fix.** New **`environment/mountains.ts`**: `buildMountains(scene, placements, track)` — one `InstancedMesh` of a unit `ConeGeometry(1,1,8)`, `MeshLambertMaterial` mapped with a procedural 256×256 `CanvasTexture` gradient (brown base → grey → white snow cap, BTK environment.js:301-306 ported verbatim); Lambert shading + the scene's existing distance fog naturally turn these into hazy silhouettes with no extra haze pass. Per-instance transform is `compose(pos(x, 0, z), yaw, scale(radius, height, radius))` off `generateMountainPlacements`; the geometry is translated `(0, 0.5, 0)` first so its base sits at local y=0 (unit cone default spans −0.5..+0.5) — same base-at-ground convention as the Stage-3 tree-trunk fix, chosen over the plan's literal "pos y=0 + unscaled unit cone" reading, which would have buried half of every mountain below y=0. Added `rotationY` to `MountainPlacement`/`generateMountainPlacements` (seeded, same stream) purely so the cone's 8 flat side-facets don't all face the camera identically across every peak. New **`environment/clouds.ts`**: `buildClouds(scene, cfg, placements, track)` — one `InstancedMesh` of a unit `PlaneGeometry(1, 0.55)`, `ShaderMaterial` porting BTK environment.js:403-428's 5-octave value-noise `cloudHash`/`cloudNoise`/`cloudFbm`/`cloudAlpha` verbatim (minus `#include <logdepthbuf_*>` — this renderer has no log depth buffer — and no `customDepthMaterial` port, since nothing in this game casts shadows); per-instance `aSeed`/`aOpacity` attributes (`aOpacity` is `DynamicDrawUsage`, rewritten every frame for edge fade); `frustumCulled = false` (clouds drift/wrap far from their initial bounds) and `renderOrder = 1` (sky dome is `-1`, both `depthWrite:false`, so clouds always draw over the dome). No true camera-facing billboard math, matching BTK's own approach exactly (identity rotation + `side: DoubleSide`) — confirmed by reading BTK's source that it never actually billboards despite the plan's phrasing, it just relies on `DoubleSide` so the fixed quad stays visible from the game's roughly-fixed −Z sight line. `buildClouds` returns `update(dt, windVec)`: drifts each cloud by `windVec.{x,z} * driftFactor * dt`, wraps toroidally via a new `wrapToField` pure helper, and recomputes edge-fade opacity via a new `cloudEdgeOpacity` pure helper — both ported verbatim from BTK's `EnvironmentRenderer.wrapToField`/`cloudEdgeOpacity` static methods into **`environment/environment-config.ts`** (plus a new `getCloudField(cfg)` deriving the wrap box's center/half-extents from `cfg.clouds.fieldZNearM/fieldZFarM/fieldHalfWidthM`, shared by `clouds.ts` so build and update agree on the same box). Locked decision (plan): clouds drift with the **dialed mean wind**, not the engine wind field — no extra engine dependency, and clouds respond the instant the player dials wind. **`environment/index.ts`**: wires `buildMountains(scene, generateMountainPlacements(cfg), track)` and `buildClouds(scene, cfg, generateCloudPlacements(cfg), track)` in after ground cover; `EnvironmentHandle.update(dt, timeS, windVec)` is no longer a no-op — it now calls `clouds.update(dt, windVec)` every frame (mountains are static, nothing to update). This animates in-game via ScopeView's Stage-1 `range?.update?.(dt, st.t, meanWindVec())` hook (already wired, no ScopeView changes needed this stage) and in the DevTools "Test Range" harness via `RangeView.tsx`'s fixed test wind vector (already wired from Stage 3). **Tests +4** (`environment-config.test.ts`): "clouds all sit inside their configured field box" (x/z/y bounds, matching the existing mountain-ring-distance test's pattern); a new `describe('cloud field wrap/fade math')` block covering `getCloudField` (derived box matches config near/far exactly), `wrapToField` (in-box value passes through unchanged; an out-of-box value teleports to the opposite edge, still inside the box), `cloudEdgeOpacity` (1.0 at field center, 0.0 exactly at an edge). `mountains.ts`/`clouds.ts` themselves get no new tests — same untestable-under-node-env-vitest class as `trees.ts`/`ground-cover.ts` (`document.createElement('canvas')`/WebGL-shader-dependent), and their placement data is already fully covered by the pure-function tests above. **Verified HERE:** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx vitest run` **342/342 green** (338 + 4 new); `npm run build` clean (same two pre-existing benign warnings: WASM glue `node:module` externalization, >500 kB chunk). **OWNER CHECK (dev server, DevTools "Test Range" tab, then in-game) — plan's Stage 4 verify list:** (1) tests green (done above); (2) clouds drift with the dialed wind direction/speed in-game, wrap without popping (edge fade), read correctly against the gradient dome; (3) snow-capped hazy ridge visible behind the forest, scene composition reads with depth (woods → hill → mountains → clouds); (4) mirage toggle ON still renders dome/clouds correctly (mirage samples the rendered frame, should be transparent to it); (5) full-loop regression on all three ranges (Range A, Sight-In, Test Range) — nothing outside `range/environment/` touched this stage; (6) **owner iPad pass**: install PWA, offline relaunch, smooth frame rate with mountains+clouds added to the existing tree/bush/rock/grass load, full shoot loop, environment sign-off. STOP — owner re-check on device required; this closes `Design/archive/test-range-environment-plan.md`'s last stage. **OWNER FEEDBACK 2026-07-21 ("We may need to darken the mountains a bit but can we adjust the zoom to go down to 0 rather than the 4.5 minimum?"):** two changes, one environment tuning + one scope-wide change outside this plan's scope. (1) **Mountain darkening** — `environment/mountains.ts`'s `buildSnowGradientTexture` gradient stops darkened ~25% on the brown base (`#6b5d4f`→`#4f453a`) and grey (`#8a8a8a`→`#6e6e6e`) stops; the white snow cap stop is unchanged. First-pass tuning, easy to adjust further from the next screenshot. (2) **Zoom floor** — literal 0× isn't physically possible: `scope-projection.ts`'s `fovRadForMag = BASE_FOV_RAD / magnification` (also `ScopeView.tsx`'s camera FOV and reticle-geometry calls) divides by magnification, so 0× is an infinite FOV — asked the owner to pick a floor; **chose 1.0× (true unaided-eye view)**. Changed the two duplicated constants that were meant to stay in sync (`scope-projection.ts:24` `SCOPE_MAG_MIN`, `state/store.ts:145` `ZOOM_MIN`) from `4.5` to `1.0`, both now commented explaining why 0 is impossible; `SCOPE_MAG_MAX`/`ZOOM_MAX` (35) untouched. `DEFAULT_MAGNIFICATION` (10) is a separate constant, unaffected — new sessions still start at 10×. Existing tests using `4.5` as a sample magnification value (`reticle.test.ts`, `scope-projection.test.ts`) are unaffected — they exercise the FOV/reticle math at an arbitrary in-range value, not the literal minimum, and 4.5 is still comfortably inside `[1.0, 35]`; `state.test.ts`'s `ZOOM_MIN` assertion references the constant symbolically, not the old literal value. **Verified HERE:** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx vitest run` **342/342 green** (no test changes needed); `npm run build` clean (same two pre-existing benign warnings). STOP — owner re-check on device required (mountains should read a bit less bright/saturated at distance; the zoom slider should now go down to 1× rather than 4.5×). **OWNER FEEDBACK 2026-07-21 ("That's a great zoom allowance. Yes, mountains need to be darker. Also the MIL/MOA marks need to adjust, for now if the zoom goes below 3x, keep the crosshair but remove all the hash marks and numbers."):** zoom floor confirmed good, no further change; two new items. (1) **Mountains darker again** — round 1's darkening apparently wasn't enough; `environment/mountains.ts`'s gradient pushed further: brown base `#4f453a`→`#332b23`, grey `#6e6e6e`→`#4d4d4d`, and this round also dims the snow cap off pure white to `#dcdcdc` (kept literal white before) so it doesn't blow out now that the body beneath it is darker. Still easy to tune again from a screenshot. (2) **Hide hash marks/numbers below 3× magnification, keep the crosshair** — added `RETICLE_HASH_MIN_MAG = 3` to **`scope/reticle.ts`**; `buildReticle` gained an optional 5th param `magnification` (default `Infinity`, so every existing call site keeps showing hash marks unless it explicitly opts in) — below the threshold it returns `ticksX`/`ticksY` as empty arrays instead of building the tick cadence. `scope/ScopeView.tsx`'s one call site (`drawReticle`) now passes the live `mag` through; **no other change needed there** — the crosshair stadia lines and centre aiming dot were already drawn unconditionally, separately from the `geo.ticksX`/`geo.ticksY` loops, so an empty tick array naturally leaves just the crosshair with zero new branching in the draw code. **Tests +2** (`reticle.test.ts`): "drops all hash marks below RETICLE_HASH_MIN_MAG, keeps them at/above it" (both units, at 2.9× and 3× exactly); "omitting magnification defaults to showing hash marks" (back-compat guard for every pre-existing call site that doesn't pass the new param). **Verified HERE:** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx vitest run` **344/344 green** (342 + 2 new); `npm run build` clean (same two pre-existing benign warnings). STOP — owner re-check on device required (mountains should read noticeably darker now; zoom below 3× should show only the crosshair, no hash marks/labels, reappearing cleanly at/above 3×). **OWNER FEEDBACK 2026-07-21 ("Still too bright.", on-device screenshot):** the screenshot showed the mountain reading as a near-uniform pale blue-white, almost indistinguishable from the sky — **ROOT CAUSE FOUND**, and it explains why two full rounds of texture darkening produced zero visible change (the same "multiplied by zero" tell as the earlier canopy bug — a value change with literally no visual effect means something else is fully overriding it). `THREE.Fog` computes `smoothstep(nearM, farM, dist)` as the FOG-color weight, and at `fog.farM: 1400` the mountain ring (`distMinM 1000`–`distMaxM 1350`) sits deep in that curve's saturated tail: ~75% fog-color at the near edge, ~99.6% at the far edge — so the rendered pixel was almost pure fog color (`0xcfe0ee`, pale sky blue) regardless of the mountain texture's actual albedo; the two darkening passes were real, correct changes that the fog was simply erasing before they ever reached the screen. **Fix — `test-range-config.ts` only** (mountain distance/height/texture untouched): raised `fog.farM` 1400→2000. This moves the mountain ring to ~43% fog at the near edge / ~71% at the far edge — a real, visible haze gradient instead of a wash, with the (already darkened) texture actually showing through now. Mountains stay at their existing distance (no size/angular-scale change, which moving them closer instead would have caused) and remain comfortably inside the new fog far and the existing 3000 m camera far. Confirmed no other element is meaningfully affected: clouds ignore `scene.fog` entirely (their own `ShaderMaterial`, ported from BTK, per the Stage-4 risk notes), the sky dome explicitly sets `fog: false` (`sky.ts:44`), and terrain/trees (max z ±500/430) sit nowhere near either the old or new far value, so their fog look is unchanged. **Verified HERE:** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx vitest run` **344/344 green** (no test changes — no test asserted the literal old `farM` value); `npm run build` clean (same two pre-existing benign warnings). STOP — owner re-check on device required (mountains should now show real shading/gradient through the haze, not a flat pale wash). **OWNER CONFIRMED 2026-07-22 ("better, that'll work" on the mountain fix; then "All looking good, let's call it done. The plan can be archived.") — Stage 4 COMPLETE. `Design/archive/test-range-environment-plan.md` CLOSED**, all 4 stages built and owner-confirmed on device across this and the prior three sessions' feedback rounds. Moved to `Design/archive/test-range-environment-plan.md` (status line updated to DONE) — completed, not superseded, so archived alongside a new README.md section distinguishing it from the retired-mid-sequence increment-plan docs already there. `Design/feature-catalog.md`'s "Test Range" entry updated to close it out (also notes the two scope-wide side effects that landed while tuning this range: zoom floor 4.5×→1×, reticle hash marks hidden below 3×). No further Test Range work queued; see the top-of-file summary line for what's next (2.3e / TS-C / TS-D awaiting owner, then Task 2.4). |

## Wooded Zero Range (owner-requested plan, `Design/archive/mil-zero-range-plan.md`)

> **CLOSED 2026-07-26** — all five stages built, iterated through owner feedback,
> and confirmed on device ("All of that checks out"). The plan doc is archived at
> `Design/archive/mil-zero-range-plan.md`. Remaining outside this section: the git
> commits (owner-side, agent cannot write to `.git`), and the Data Book "not
> zeroed" note that D16 needs from task 2.4f.

Origin: owner asked (2026-07-26) for a new sight-in range at 25/50/100/200 with
the existing zeroing targets, in the Test Range's wooded style. Design question
answered first: shooter elevation above the targets is **ballistically free** —
`error ~= g*H^2/(4*v0^2)`, independent of target distance (10 m of elevation
shifts impact 0.36 mm), so the firing point sits on a low knoll and occlusion is
solved geometrically rather than by spreading targets wide. Five stages,
protocol-sized, STOP after each.

| Stage | Status | Date | Commit | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **DONE — owner confirmed 2026-07-26** | 2026-07-26 | (pending — agent cannot commit, see queue) | **Registry + pure config + corridor model. No THREE, no scene, no ScopeView.** `range/ranges.ts`: new `TargetKind` capability (`'paper' \| 'steel'`) on `RangeDefinition` — the field that lets the paper-bay fire path / zeroing flow / Clean / Inspect stop testing `sceneType === 'sight-in'` (plan §7.2); optional `RangeStation.azimuthDeg` for fanned bays (`side`'s three buckets are too coarse); `'wooded-zero'` added to `RangeSceneType`; `WOODED_ZERO` row (4 stations at 25/50/100/200, azimuths −6.0/−2.0/+1.5/+4.5, `targetKind: 'paper'`, `zeroable: true`, `unitCharacter: 'both'`). New **`range/wooded-zero-config.ts`**: knoll profile (`corridorFloorY`, 1.5 m crest at 10% grade), LOS-range↔ground-run conversion (`groundRunForLosRange` — station distance is line-of-sight, NOT ground run, since that is what the solver must receive), the terminating-wedge corridor model (`buildCorridors`, `insideAnyCorridor`, `isPlantable`, `corridorHalfWidth`), board/marker sizing (`boardSizeFor`, constant 12 mrad subtension with a face-size floor), and `snapshotWoodedZero` on the existing D3 entry-snapshot contract. **32 tests** in `range/wooded-zero-config.test.ts` + 2 added to `ranges.test.ts`. New pure `generateRidgeProfile(cfg, layerIndex)` in `environment-config.ts` builds a crest polyline by sweeping a summed-sinusoid height profile across the arc — seeded phases, non-integer octave frequencies so it never repeats, smooth by construction (no value-noise sampling artefacts at low segment counts). `mountains.ts` rewritten to build one triangle strip per layer between the crest and a base carried to `RIDGE_BASE_Y_M = -40` (below the eye's horizon — the ridges sit ~1 km out, far past the terrain mesh, so nothing else fills the gap under them). Material is **unlit `MeshBasicMaterial`** on purpose: at a kilometre-plus shading detail is invisible, and a flat colour makes the rendered pixel exactly `mix(ridgeColor, fogColor, fogFactor)` — the predictability that the old cone-plus-gradient-texture setup lacked when two rounds of darkening produced no visible change. Both configs get two layers (1100 m dark/tall, 1430 m pale/low), landing at ~49% and ~69% haze. Bend is **quadratic in height above the trunk base** (a cantilever: crowns move, trunks do not), capped at `maxBendM` so a crown cannot lean into a shooting corridor past the 1.5 m canopy placement margin. **The sampler passed in is `windAtForMarkers`** — already "the wind this range's shots actually experience" (full field on a steel range, dialled mean on a paper bay, each matching its own solve), so vegetation and bullet read the SAME wind by construction. That is the entire justification for the feature; if they ever diverge it stops being worth having. `EnvironmentHandle.update` / `SteelSceneApi.update` / `PaperBayScene.update` all gained the optional 4th `sampleWindAt` arg. **Grass deliberately NOT swayed** — plan §9.6 originally gave tufts a HIGHER bend factor, but §7.3 (owner) superseded that with "tree tops only", and a tuft thrashing under a breeze the crown barely notices would contradict the height story. **Stage 4b — owner feedback round 1 (2026-07-26, on device).** One cosmetic ask
and one REAL BUG:

1. *"The markers above the targets have the distance number but not the
   measurement."* Plate now reads `25 M` / `100 YD` — `markerText` + `unitLabel`
   added to `WoodedZeroStation` (pure/testable), and the canvas font is fitted to
   the available WIDTH rather than a fraction of plate height, since "200 YD" is
   twice the glyphs of "25" and a height-derived size overflows at the long
   stations. `M`/`YD` matches the original Zero Range's distance signs so the two
   bays read alike.
2. *"The targets are pointing straight ahead and not directly at the shooter, the
   25 is the most obvious."* **Genuine sign error.** `WoodedZeroScene.addStation`
   computed `yaw = atan2(s.x, -s.z)`, which is `+azimuth`; the correct facing is
   `atan2(-s.x, -s.z)` = `-azimuth`. Every board was therefore yawed the WRONG WAY,
   with a visible error of **twice** the station azimuth: 12 deg at 25 m, 9 deg at
   200 m, 4 deg at 50 m, 3 deg at 100 m — and the owner correctly identified 25 m
   as the worst. Fixed by moving the maths into a pure `facingYawRad(station)` in
   `wooded-zero-config.ts` (derived from the POSITIONS, not the azimuth, so it
   survives a placement-convention change) with 4 tests, including one that
   asserts the wrong formula differs from the right one by exactly 2x azimuth so a
   future "simplification" back to `atan2(x, -z)` cannot pass.

**Owner question answered:** yes, station distances follow the unit preference —
`snapshotWoodedZero(unitsPrimary)` gives 25/50/100/200 **metres** under MIL and
25/50/100/200 **yards** under MOA (plus the matching MIL/MOA target face, D7).
The layout is snapshotted at range ENTRY (D3), so flipping units mid-session does
not move the targets — you have to leave and re-enter the range. Asserted in
`wooded-zero-config.test.ts`.

Gates after round 1: vitest 45 files / **537** tests green, `tsc --noEmit` clean,
`vite build` green.

**Stage 4a — latent Stage 2b defect found by a shifted PRNG stream.** Adding
per-tree randomness (aspect, tilt, variant) moved every subsequent draw, which
rearranged the forest and turned the Stage 2b test *"puts woods behind the near
stations"* red. Investigated rather than reseeded: the property was **genuinely
false**, and had been all along — the 25 m and 50 m stations had **zero** trees
within 60 m behind them. The old test only passed by luck of the previous
stream, because it asserted `> 0` on a set that happened to contain one.

Root cause is not the seed. Tree bands are sampled uniformly by area, and the
near-station backdrop pocket is ~4% of the big first band's footprint — most of
which corridor rejection then removes. The design promised "each station sits in
its own pocket of forest" (plan §3.2) and the placement never delivered it; a
Stage 1 point-sample test asking whether a tree COULD stand there is a different
question from whether one DOES.

Fixed by adding a dedicated near-station backdrop band
(`x -45..10, z -125..-32`) which reaches LEFT of the 25 m lane, because the wedge
between adjacent near lanes is too narrow for a canopy — the woods sit just
outside the fan and still read as backdrop at that distance. Backdrop counts now
19 / 18 / 12 trees behind the 25/50/100 m stations (was 0 / 0 / 4), and the test
asserts DENSITY rather than existence so it cannot pass on luck again. The 200 m
station stays deliberately clearer near-in (its heavy block sits at z < -250 per
the >=40 m miss rule) and has its own assertion saying so.

**Stage 3 — owner feedback round 1 (2026-07-26, on device).** Two small asks,
both taken:

1. *"Make the sun a bit higher, an hour or so later, it's a bit darker than I'd
   like."* Elevation **14 -> 24 deg** (the sun climbs ~10-13 deg/hour at
   mid-latitudes, so about an hour), sun intensity **1.35 -> 1.6**, hemisphere
   fill **0.55 -> 0.75**, key warmed off deep orange (`0xffd9a3 -> 0xffe3ba`)
   since a higher sun is less reddened. Checked the two constraints this could
   have broken: board illumination barely moves (0.557 -> 0.524, still well above
   the 0.4 floor) and shadows shorten 4.0x -> 2.25x object height, still clearly
   raking versus the ~0.7x of the old midday rig. Relaxed two test bounds that
   were pinned to the original angle, with comments saying what they actually
   guard (drift back toward midday, not a specific hour).
2. *"There are flags for wind, remove them."* Done as a **capability**, not
   another `sceneType` disjunction: new `RangeDefinition.windMarkers`. Range A and
   the original Zero Range keep flags; the Test Range (calm sandbox) and the
   Wooded Zero Range (token breeze only) do not. This also retires the last item
   on the plan §7.2 coupling table — `ScopeView`'s `markerSpecs` no longer tests
   `sceneType === 'test-range'`. Wind CONTROLS are deliberately left in the HUD:
   the player can still dial wind, and from Stage 5 the canopy becomes the
   indicator the flags used to be, which is the whole point of §9.6.

Gates after round 1: vitest 43 files / **490** tests green, `tsc --noEmit` clean,
`vite build` green.

**Stage 2b — owner feedback round 1 (2026-07-26, on device).** Two defects, both
in the target furniture; the bay geometry itself (fan, knoll, occlusion, woods)
was not questioned.

1. *"The paper target is placed too high and overs the distance marker."* The
   lane-number plate was drawn into a SQUARE texture on a SQUARE board, so it
   landed wherever the border left room — and the paper face, centred on the aim
   point at 1.0 m, covered its lower half. Fixed by making the board an explicit
   RECTANGLE: a square paper region centred on 1.0 m with a reserved plate band
   stacked above it (`boardLayoutFor`), and a canvas whose aspect ratio matches
   the board so the plate maps to exactly that band. The paper did NOT move — the
   board grew upward instead, because every sight-line and occlusion proof in the
   plan is drawn from a target centre at exactly 1.0 m. New test asserts
   `plateBottom > paperTop` in both unit systems.
2. *"The board ... is comically large at the 200 meter mark. The boards should
   all be the same size."* Reverses the constant-angular-subtension rule
   (`0.012 x losRange`, which made the 200 m board **2.40 m** wide) in favour of
   a constant physical size derived from the face: `width = FACE x 1.5`,
   `plate = FACE x 0.4`. The 200 m board is now **0.66 m**. Recomputed the §4
   occlusion table: worst margin **+2.00 deg metric / +1.21 deg imperial**, i.e.
   equal boards are slightly BETTER at the binding 100/200 pair than the scaled
   ones were (+2.49 vs +2.03) — the far boards shrink faster than the near ones
   grow. Plan §4 and §5.1 rewritten; the superseded rule is recorded there rather
   than deleted.

Gates after round 1: vitest 42 files / **467** tests green, `tsc --noEmit` clean,
`vite build` green.

**Stage 1 defect, found and fixed in 2a:** Stage 1 added `WOODED_ZERO` to
`listRanges()` before it had a scene builder. `RangeSelect` renders one card per
listed range and D8 forbids grayed-out "coming soon" slots, so selecting it fell
through ScopeView's scene branch to the steel `RangeScene` — the player would get
Range A's world under a "Wooded Zero" label. Fixed by splitting the registry into
`RANGES` (landing-screen order) and `UNLISTED` (resolvable by id, not shown);
`BY_ID` merges both. A range joins `RANGES` only when its scene exists, which
Stage 2b will do. Guarded by two tests in `paper-bay-scene.test.ts`.

**The invariant Stage 1 exists to protect (plan §8):** a yard is shorter than a
metre at every nominal distance (200 yd = 182.88 m < 200 m), so the METRIC
layout's corridors are a strict superset of the imperial layout's. The world —
terrain, forest, corridors — is therefore built ONCE from the metric station set,
and imperial targets sit short on the same lane axes. `buildCorridors()`
deliberately takes no unit argument so it cannot accidentally become
unit-dependent; four tests in the `DUAL-UNIT SUPERSET INVARIANT` block assert it
directly. If someone later regenerates the environment from the imperial station
set, the woods shift between unit modes and the guarantee is gone.

**Architectural changes made under this plan (owner-approved via the plan doc,
2026-07-26)** — flagged per execution-protocol §6, which requires stopping before
architectural choices the build-plan does not cover:
- `TargetKind` capability added to `RangeDefinition` (Stage 1, done).
- `PaperBayScene` interface to be extracted in Stage 2 — `SightInScene` already
  implements every method; the change is declaring the interface and typing
  ScopeView's local against it instead of the concrete class.
- Stage 3 enables `renderer.shadowMap` globally for the first time (today every
  `castShadow` flag in the codebase is inert). This affects every range, not just
  this one.


## ELR Probe (owner-requested plan, `Design/archive/elr-probe-plan.md`)

Origin: owner asked (2026-07-27) for a long-range DOPE range out to ~3000. Design work
established the ceiling (**.50 BMG, supersonic to 2267 m / 2479 yd**) and produced
`Design/elr-dope-range-plan.md` — then the owner correctly called for a **throwaway probe
first**: *"a very simple test of flat ground, gongs every 500 to 3k … learn what works and
doesn't and then build on that."* The full plan is now banner-marked UNVALIDATED and the
probe is the live build. Two scenes: **Probe A** (dead flat) and **Probe B** (10 m bluff over
a convex rising slope). Steps P0–P3, STOP after each.

### ✅ PROBE CLOSED (2026-07-28) — it answered every Tier-1 question

Findings live in **`Design/archive/elr-probe-plan.md` §5.0**; the consequences are written
into `Design/elr-dope-range-plan.md` (rewritten 2026-07-28 — the banners are gone, see P18). Headline: TOF is fine,
iPad holds 60 fps, depth is 24-bit, **the convex slope wins** (retiring the bluff + fan),
and trace/impact/ping all read at 2000 m. The plan doc is archived. **The probe CODE is now
deleted too (2026-07-29)** — see the retention note below.

**Open items that OUTLIVE the probe** (recorded here because the plan doc is now archived):

| item | state | where it goes next |
|---|---|---|
| **~30 ms frame spikes on iPad** | Mean is inside the 16 ms budget; the spikes are unattributed. Suspects cheapest-first: the per-shot solve (a 3 km fine table is thousands of integration steps on the main thread), the wind-field solve, plate-atlas writes on impact. | Attribute **before** the real range adds terrain and vegetation — the probe is deliberately empty, so this is the floor, not the ceiling. |
| **Intermittent FIRE failure** | Not root-caused. Reporting fixed in P5 (`describeThrown`), so the next occurrence names itself. Engine throw sites tabled in the archived §5.0b. | Waiting on one more on-device reproduction. |
| **`computeZero`'s hard-coded 5 s wall** | Found while reading; a second ceiling P0 did not reach. Harmless at short zeros, fatal for the no-gear sight-in fallback (`zeroRangeM: distanceM`) at long range. | Logged in `Wiki/_gaps.md` (Phase-2 engine tasks). |
| **Probe code retention** | **RESOLVED 2026-07-29 — deleted.** Both probe ranges (flat + slope) and `ELRProbeScene.ts` / `elr-probe-config.ts` / `elr-probe-trees.ts` (+ tests), `listUnlistedRanges()` and the DIAGNOSTIC strip in `RangeSelect.tsx` are gone. | Condition met: the ELR range has its own tested equivalents — the convex profile and LOS→ground-run solve live in `elr-range-config.ts`, the occlusion search in `sight-clearance.ts`. Survivors the ELR range uses: `bullseye-texture.ts`, `scope/perf-hud.ts`, `scope/miss-projection.ts`, `scope/phase-timer.ts`, `scope/aim-pick.ts`, and `SteelSceneApi`'s `eyeHeightM` / `groundYAt`. |

| **ELR-POLISH-1a** | **DONE (gates) — AWAITING OWNER on device** | 2026-07-29 | (pending, owner-side) | **Grass tuft field reshaped — the hard cutoff the owner saw is gone.** Owner on device: *"the tufts only show close to the shooter but die off quickly, just shortly after the 50 yard… Perhaps the ground plane is rising but the grass is not?"* **Not the terrain** — `groundY(80)` is 0.14 m and the tufts were sitting on it correctly (there is a test). It was my `GRASS_ZONE_M = 80` boundary: **two faults, both mine.** (1) 80 m was sized off "when does a tuft stop being worth drawing", which is the wrong question on a range where the near ground is most of the picture and the low line shoots to 500 m. (2) **A uniform-density field always ends in a hard line**, and grass that stops dead across the whole view reads as a rendering fault, not as the edge of a field. **Fix — a tapered SECTOR instead of a rectangle:** full density to `GRASS_FULL_M = 150`, then density falling linearly to zero at `GRASS_FADE_M = 500` (the low line's last station, where a 0.21 m tuft subtends **0.42 mrad** — sub-pixel at 1×, so the field runs out exactly where the individual tuft stops being resolvable). A sector at ±25° rather than a rectangle because a band wide enough at 500 m is absurdly wide at 50 m; ±25° is far outside the ±2° station fan and covers a 1× field of view. Sampling is **area-uniform** (`r = √u·FADE`) with the taper applied as a rejection — sampling `r` uniformly would pile tufts at the shooter's feet, which is where they are least wanted. **Measured profile** (tufts/m² by 50 m band): 0.19 · 0.21 · **0.22** · 0.20 · 0.17 · 0.14 · 0.11 · 0.077 · 0.047 · **0.016** — flat at the Wooded Zero Range's density through the full band, then a smooth decay with no cliff. Count **1600 → 11 000**, derived rather than picked: 0.216 /m² over the taper-weighted sector area (≈ 50 800 m²). Still ONE draw call, ~55 000 triangles, unlit, no shadow pass — the ELR range's perf HUD (`render ms · calls · tris`) is right there to check it rather than assume. **Second defect found while fixing the first, and it would have been reported as a bug:** a tuft scales to **0.29 m** tall and the 50 m gong is **5 cm** across with its centre ~0.25 m up on a 12" stake, so grass could simply hide the near targets — and `sight-clearance.ts` only knows about trees. New `rejectTuftsAtStations()` drops tufts within 5 m of a station (generous: from the prone eye the sight line to a 50 m stake is still 0.54 m up at 40 m, so a tuft only reaches it within ~2 m of the target). Applied in the scene against the ACTIVE line's layout, exactly as `cullTreeIndices` already is, so the tuft field stays line-independent — **measured cost: 105 tufts removed on the low line, 15 on the high line**, out of 11 000. Gates: `tsc --noEmit` clean · vitest **822 passed / 56 files** (16 in `elr-range-cover.test.ts`, including a density-falls-monotonically test and a regression asserting a majority of the field lies past the old 80 m edge) · `npm run build` green. |

We also need to add in the tufts of grass."*). **Ground:** `ELRRangeScene.addGround()` swapped its `MeshStandardMaterial({ color: GROUND_HEX })` for `loadPbrMaterial('textures/grass/Grass004_1K-JPG')` — the same call, the same texture set and the same **8 m tile** the Wooded Zero Range uses, now pinned as `GROUND_TEXTURE_TILE_M` with the reason written down: apparent blade scale is what makes two ranges read as the same place, and a bigger tile to cut the repeat count would make the low line's grass look like a different species. On 1400 × 3000 m that is a **175 × 375 repeat — both integers**, so the wrap is seamless; mipmaps + the loader's 4× anisotropy carry the distance. `GROUND_HEX` is now only the offline-first fallback and was moved from dark olive `0x6d7355` to the Wooded Zero Range's own `0x7d9450`, so a failed texture load degrades identically on both ranges instead of one going dark. **Tufts:** new pure `range/elr-range-cover.ts` (**9 tests**) generating placements, with the MESH coming from the shared `environment/ground-cover.ts` — `buildGrassTuftMesh` was un-privated for this, so the tuft geometry, the dark-base/light-tip vertex gradient and the unlit material are literally the Wooded Zero Range's rather than a lookalike. Called directly instead of `buildGroundCover` so the ELR range does not build (and texture-load) bush and rock meshes it has no placements for. Placement rules, each sized rather than guessed: **1600 tufts** — chosen to match that range's *density* (~0.22/m² over its eligible ground), not its count, so the ground reads the same up close; **80 m** deep and **±50 m** wide, sized by visibility (a 0.21 m tuft subtends 3.5 mrad at 60 m and is a sub-pixel speck much past 100 m) and deeper than the Wooded Zero Range's 34 m because the ELR low line shoots 50–500 m over that near ground; **18 m clear radius**, held at the Wooded Zero Range's value because it is an *optics* constraint — a tuft at 3 m fills the sight picture at 20× — and deliberately NOT the trees' 30 m `FIRING_POINT_CLEAR_RADIUS_M`, which would strip grass out of the most-looked-at ground on the range to clear something 0.2 m tall. Own seed (`GRASS_SEED`, distinct from `TREE_SEED`, asserted) so adding or removing a tree cannot reshuffle the grass, and `y = groundY(-z)` so tufts sit on the convex profile. One `InstancedMesh`, ~8 000 triangles, one draw call. **No dirt apron added** (the Wooded Zero Range has one): the convex crest hides the far ground edge — that is what the profile is for — and 4 000 trees cover the lateral edges, so there is no visible seam to skirt. Worked around in TS (stake plates **bolted**, `chainOutwardOffsetFor()` clamp that is a no-op above ~35 cm so no shipped range changed); logged in `Wiki/_gaps.md`, owner-triaged low priority. (4) **the .22 DOPE card showed ONE ROW** — `assembleComeUp` trimmed at the first subsonic row, but both catalog .22 LR loads are subsonic *at the muzzle by design*, so it pushed one row and broke; the trim now only applies to loads that had a supersonic phase. **Owner-requested changes:** ammo lots 20 → **100 rounds**; the 3-shot budget is **gone** (`DEFAULT_SHOT_BUDGET = Infinity`, COMMIT retained, counter hidden when non-finite, finite per-range budgets still honoured); DOPE card gained a **transonic footnote** explaining what its amber/red banding means, worded to claim reduced trust and NOT that groups open (gap N4 — drag rise is modelled, dispersion is not). **Task 10 correction:** the spec's Done-when says a 6.5 CM reads TRANSONIC at 1250 m; measured against the engine it is Mach 1.195 at **1000 m** and Mach 0.944 at **1300 m**, so 1250 m is already subsonic — plan §11 agrees, the spec was wrong, and the built code follows the plan. ⚠ **Stale owner-side commit queues below (probe P0/P2) name `Design/elr-dope-range-plan.md` at its old path** — it now lives in `Design/archive/`. Gates at archive time: `tsc --noEmit` clean · vitest **844 passed / 57 files** · `npm run build` green · golden vectors **36 cases, worst rel diff 0.000e+0**. |
| P0 | **DONE — gates green, awaiting owner go-ahead for P1** | 2026-07-27 | (pending — agent cannot commit, see queue) | **Lifted the solver's hardcoded 10-second flight cap.** `engine-bridge/index.ts:148` called `sim.simulate(maxRangeM * 1.05, dt, 10.0)`; the third argument is **max time of flight**, and `Simulator::simulate` (`engine/src/ballistics/simulator.cpp:372–386`) exits on **whichever comes first, distance or time**. Invisible at 500 yd; binding at 3 km. **Measured against the engine itself, the bug was worse than the plan's estimate**: nominal 15 °C calm gives TOF 9.45 s to 3000 m (0.55 s of margin), but **0 °C with a 15 mph headwind is 10.18 s** and the old code returned **five rows, stopping at 2500 m** — the 3000 m station silently had NO firing solution, with no error and no gap, so a caller could not distinguish a truncated solve from a legitimate one. Colder/slower/bulk-lot variants run to 10.83 s. Fix: new optional `SolveOptions.maxTimeS` + exported `DEFAULT_MAX_TIME_S = 30`, documented at length with the measured numbers so the constant cannot be quietly re-tightened. | P17 | **Rimfire ladder sized; two corrections to my own analysis** | 2026-07-28 | (pending, owner-side) | **Owner caught two errors in the .22 write-up, both of which I had stated too confidently.** (1) *"Upper limit ~300 m"* assumed **dial-only** — the same mistake the main plan already corrects for the ELR ladder, where holdover is explicitly a technique rather than a gear gate. With ~15 MIL of holdover (available at 10× on an FFP reticle) **500 m / 547 yd is reachable**: 34.56 MIL come-up, 5.16 MIL wind. Matches the owner's real-world reading of 200–400 yd "with a LOT of holdover". (2) *"The .22 has no transonic wall"* is true only of the catalog's standard-velocity load. **High-velocity .22 LR leaves at Mach 1.12 and is back through Mach 1 by 50 m** — it crosses the transonic band right in front of the shooter. Full three-load table (standard / high-vel / .22 WMR) now in the plan. **The interesting consequence is a modelling gap, logged as `Wiki/_gaps.md` N4:** HV shoots flatter (29.39 vs 34.56 MIL at 500 m) and in reality groups *worse*, which is why rimfire competitors buy subsonic match ammo — but dispersion here comes only from MV SD, BC SD and rifle precision, none of which know about Mach. So in-game the faster round is strictly better, i.e. a correct-looking model teaching the opposite of the truth. Same mechanism underlies the Mach 1.2 transonic-onset threshold that sizes the whole ELR range, so it is not a rimfire curiosity. **Oracle extended 300 → 500 m for `22lr-40-standard`** — reachability verified first (10/10 rows, TOF 1.98–2.26 s), **636 pre-existing rows bit-identical, 24 added, 660 total, 0.000e+0**. Do not push past 500: this load truncates silently and by a different amount per atmosphere. **Owner decision: ONE terrain, TWO firing points** — low rimfire line plus elevated ELR line, because from the ELR eye a 50 m target sits 12° below, which is a strange stance and adds cosine error to the stations meant to teach clean drop. 250 m becomes a shared station, making the 4:1 scaling visible on one plate. |
| P16 | **Layout is seed-deterministic; solve cost cut 4.6×** | 2026-07-28 | (pending, owner-side) | **Owner asked whether the tree layout is saved or random. Neither — it is regenerated identically every time.** `mulberry32` seeded with a hard-coded constant, and `withSolvedOffsets` is a pure function of the resulting field, so the same 4000 trees AND the same station offsets come back on every entry, every device, every reinstall, with nothing persisted. **The seed is the save file.** Caveat recorded because it will bite later: the layout is *reproducible*, not *pinned* — any change to the generator (scale range, conifer fraction, ground extent, or even the ORDER of `rand()` calls) reshuffles the forest and moves the solved offsets with it. Low stakes for DOPE, since a row is keyed to DISTANCE and lateral offset does not affect it, but a range the owner has learned would quietly rearrange. If the real range wants permanence, bake the solved offsets into data rather than recomputing. **Perf bug found while answering this**: the solve cost **470 ms desktop (~1.4 s on iPad)**, paid on every range entry — `boundsOf` was being derived inside the candidate loops, so `treeUnitBounds`'s array maps ran ~2.9 M times per 6-station solve. New `Occluder` + `prepareOccluders()` flatten the forest once; `chooseOffset`, `occludingTreeIndices` and `clearanceMarginM` now take either shape via `asOccluders`. **470 → 102 ms desktop, ~322 ms iPad, with byte-identical output** (offsets unchanged: −7.9, +10.0, −1.3, −48.3, −6.2, +39.9). A broad-phase corridor cull would take it further if range-entry time ever matters. Gates: vitest **751 passed / 55 files**, `tsc --noEmit` clean. |
| P15 | **Solver WIRED INTO the probe — targets now sited by the forest** | 2026-07-28 | (pending, owner-side) | **P14 built the solver but never connected it; the scene still used fixed placement.** Now wired, and the ordering is the whole trick: `ELRProbeScene` generates the tree field **first**, knowing nothing about targets, then re-sites the stations into whatever gaps that forest leaves (`withSolvedOffsets`). The old order — keep trees out of the sight lines — is what made the first clearance study circular and what produced a mown corridor. **The 45 m lane and the 25 m per-target rings are gone**; the only exclusion left is 30 m around the shooter. **Tree count stays SELECTABLE** (owner asked): safe because the field is generated once at `MAX_TREES` and each ramp step draws a *prefix*, so offsets solved against the full 4000 hold at every smaller step — no re-solving, no gongs jumping around mid-sweep. Tested explicitly (`full.slice(0, n)` equals `generate(n)` at every step), because if prefix-stability ever broke the targets would silently end up behind trees. `OFFSET_CAP_MRAD = 25` from the P14 knee — angular, not metric, same rule as the 1 MIL gongs. **The result is a strong second argument for the convex slope**, beyond "looks better": against the same 4000-tree field, flat needs 4/5/10/12/15 trees cut at 1000–3000 m, while the **slope comes out completely clear at every station and every density** (one occluder in the whole range, at 500 m). A level sight line meets every tree between shooter and target; a rising one climbs away from the canopy. **Expect flat to show occluded gongs as you ramp — that is the finding, not a bug.** Gates: vitest **751 passed / 55 files**, `tsc --noEmit` clean. |
| P14 | **Sight-line clearance solver — "put targets among the trees"** | 2026-07-28 | (pending, owner-side) | **Answers the owner's question directly: yes, and it costs individual trees rather than a corridor.** New `range/sight-clearance.ts` (**23 tests**) + `treeUnitBounds()` exported from `environment/trees.ts`, derived from the same variant tables that build the meshes so it cannot go stale. Two design points that a naive version gets wrong: the occlusion test is a **cone**, not a ray (a ray happily accepts a trunk covering all but the centre pixel of the plate), and it checks **height as well as plan position** (on a rising slope a tree 800 m out on low ground sits under the sight line and blocks nothing — rejecting it would cost a tree for free). `chooseOffset()` inverts the authoring problem: distances are fixed on a DOPE range, so it **searches the lateral offset** for the position the forest already leaves clearest, giving irregular natural placement instead of an authored C-L-R rhythm. Offsets solve `z = -√(d² - offset²)`, or a station quietly slides off the distance it exists to teach. **Measured**, 4000 trees / 1200×2100 m / no lane / 8 seeds, angular caps: ±10 mrad → 9.5 trees cut, ±20 → 6.5, ±30 → 5.5, ±45 → 4.4; traverse at 20× runs 1.0 → 4.3 fields of view. **±20–30 mrad is the knee** — past it, extra freedom buys ~1 tree and costs a whole field of view. **Correction worth recording:** a first single-seed run reported **zero** culls at every station and was reported as such internally; across 8–20 seeds the true figure is 4–10. Single-seed results on randomised geometry are anecdotes. Also fixed 3 wrong test expectations found this way — in each case the code was right (notably: a fixed metric gap NEAR the eye is a *bigger* angular hole, not a smaller one). Gates: vitest **752 passed / 55 files**, `tsc --noEmit` clean. |
| P13 | **Tree-headroom probe — READY TO MEASURE** | 2026-07-28 | (pending, owner-side) | **Turns the tree budget from a guess into a measurement.** The gap this closes: the probe's empty scene reads 17 ms on iPad, but that is **vsync-capped**, so it is equally consistent with 8 ms of work and half a frame spare or 16.9 ms with none — and those imply wildly different budgets for a 2 km wooded range. Two additions. **(1) `perf-hud.ts` gains `RenderCostMeter` + `frameUtilisation`/`headroomVerdict`**: the render call is bracketed with `performance.now()`, which is the only cost distinguishable from waiting for the display. Honest caveat written into the source — WebGL is async, so this is CPU-side (traversal, culling, draw submission) and **understates a GPU-bound scene**; that is the right bias, because trees are a draw-call and vertex story first, and the tree ramp catches the GPU side by pushing until the capped frame time itself breaks. Read together: `render 3 / frame 17` = roomy, `render 15 / frame 17` = nothing left, `render 4 / frame 30` = already over and NOT CPU-side. **(2) `elr-probe-trees.ts` + `ELRProbeScene.setTreeCount()`**: a deterministic field over the probe's convex slope, ramped 0/250/500/1000/2000/4000 from on-screen buttons, reusing **the Wooded Zero Range's actual `buildTrees` renderer and palette** — a budget measured against stand-in geometry would be a budget for the wrong trees. Deterministic so 500-vs-1000 compares the same 500; rebuilt rather than instance-count-toggled so "0" really is an empty scene; perf windows reset on change so the build hitch does not pollute the reading. Readout: `render X ms · verdict · N calls · Nk tris · N trees`. **17 new tests.** Gates: vitest **729 passed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` green. **OWNER: ramp until it breaks, and report the step where `render` climbs or frame time leaves 17 ms.** |
| P12 | **On-device shot-cost profile (definitive)** | 2026-07-28 | (pending, owner-side) | **Clean run after a reload, first shot at each station (ms):** 500 m — total 19 (trace 11, scatter 5, solve 3); 2000 m — total 62 (scatter 55, solve 6); 3000 m — total 86 (scatter 80, solve 5). **Three things fall out.** (1) **The 3× native proxy is confirmed as a prediction, not a post-hoc fit** — 2000 m was predicted at ~56 ms from a 18 ms native measurement *before* this run and came in at **62**. Native measurement is therefore a usable stand-in for device cost, no deploy cycle needed. (2) **`scatter` is the whole story and it scales with range**, as expected — it is the per-station `MatchSimulator` zero. `solve` is flat and cheap at 3–6 ms regardless of distance, so the deterministic firing solution is not a concern at any range. (3) **The `trace 11` at 500 m is warm-up, not a trace cost** — it was the first shot after the reload, and trace does not appear in the top three at 2000 or 3000 (where it is ~1 ms), despite flying six times further. A range-dependent cost cannot get *cheaper* with range; a cold code path can. Same explanation as the earlier anomalous 107 ms at 3000 m, which this run re-measured at 80. **Net:** worst case at the ELR range's actual 2000 m cap is ~62 ms — about four frames, once per station, cached thereafter. |

Against the pre-fix engine (1111 ms native) the same shot would have cost roughly **3 seconds** on this device. **The trigger works at every station and the freeze is gone.** What remains is a ~6-frame hitch on the *first* shot at each station (owner confirmed subsequent shots at the same station read 1–2 ms, so `simAt`'s per-station cache is doing its job). | P10 | **CI red → green (both failures were the two flagged files)** | 2026-07-28 | (pending, owner-side) | **First CI run failed on exactly the two files the agent could not compile locally**, which is the useful part: the risk was named in advance and the failures landed where predicted, not somewhere unexpected. **(1) `simulator.cpp` — `-Wnan-infinity-disabled`.** The WASM branch compiles with `-O3 -ffast-math -Werror`; `-ffast-math` asserts infinities cannot occur, so *naming* one is UB and the warning is an error. `std::numeric_limits<float>::infinity()` → `::max()`; a finite "worse than any real error" sentinel does the identical job for a best-so-far tracker. Native g++ never complained because the local check was not using the CI flag set — **now it is** (`-O3 -ffast-math -Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror` over every non-embind source). **(2) `bindings.cpp` — embind cannot return `const T&` under `return_value_policy::reference`.** `wire.h` needs a non-const `T*`. The original binding had **no** policy at all, so embind copied into a heap object that JS `.delete()`s — which is exactly what `engine-bridge/index.ts` does (`sim.computeZero(...).delete()`). Dropping the policy from both new bindings restores the pre-existing semantics precisely; adding it was the mistake, and the fact that the original omitted it was the clue. Re-verified after both fixes: sources compile clean under the full CI flag set, and the native golden matrix is **still 636 rows byte-identical to pristine**. `bindings.cpp` remains the one file only CI can check. |
| P9 | **FIXED — long-zero freeze, verified natively; needs the rebuild** | 2026-07-28 | (pending, owner-side) | **The ~1 s freeze at far stations is gone — and the P8 diagnosis of it was wrong in an instructive way.** Instrumenting the iteration showed it is *not* failing to converge: it holds a clean **0.50 error ratio per step at every range**, 100 m to 3000 m. It is healthy. What it cannot do is beat **float32 noise** — the residual of a ~9,000-step trajectory bottoms out near **5e-6 m**, so `MatchSimulator`'s **1e-6 m** (one micron) tolerance is *unreachable* at long range and the loop ground out its full 1000-trial ceiling chasing arithmetic it could not deliver. So the fix is not a better root-finder, it is **knowing when to stop**: bail once six straight trials fail to beat the best error, keep the best iterate. **First attempt only half-worked** and is worth recording — comparing each step against the *previous* error let the bouncing residual near the floor keep resetting the streak, so 2500 and 3000 m stopped but 2000 m still ground out 1000 trials; comparing against the *best so far* is immune to that. Measured (tol 1e-6): **2000 m 617 → 17.8 ms, 2500 m 852 → 24.4 ms, 3000 m 1111 → 34.1 ms**, with residual quality unchanged (sub-mm at 3000 m). **Proven a no-op on the validated path, not asserted:** new `GameBuild/validation/native-matrix.cpp` (+ `README-native-matrix.md`) builds the full 36-case golden matrix from **pristine BTK** and from the **patched engine** and diffs them — **636 rows, byte-identical**, which is strictly stronger than the harness's 1e-4 tolerance and needed no emsdk. That tool is the reusable win here: any future `computeZero`-adjacent change can be cleared before paying for a rebuild. 3 more tests in `tests/test_zeroing.cpp` (early exit does not degrade the zero; short zeros still *meet* their tolerance rather than stopping early). **OWNER: rebuild + `node GameBuild/validation/run.mjs` is still the authority — native float is not WASM.** |
| P8 | **ROOT-CAUSED — fix blocked on an engine rebuild** | 2026-07-28 | (pending, owner-side) | **The dead FIRE button is a hard 2050 m wall in the hit-sim, not an intermittent fault.** Owner's device report — *"I can fire at 1500, 2000 and higher fails"* — plus `describeThrown` reporting `f thrown with no message {excPtr}` (a C++ exception object, exactly the shape the P5 fallback was written to identify) turned three sessions of guessing into a five-minute diagnosis. `MatchSimulator`'s constructor (`engine/src/match/simulator.cpp:43`) **zeroes at the target range** and so inherits `computeZero`'s hard-coded **5 s** flight wall while asking for **1.1 × target**; a 6.5 CM cannot fly 2.2 km in 5 s, so it throws. **Measured against pristine BTK** — OK at 1500/1800/1900/2000, **throws at 2100/2500/3000** — a sharp, deterministic, gear-independent wall that matches the device exactly. Recoil still plays because the kick is applied after the catch, which is why it read as a dead trigger. This is the same `computeZero` 5 s wall logged in `Wiki/_gaps.md` during P6 as "not yet shown to be the cause"; it now is. **Fix:** trailing `float max_time = 5.0f` on `computeZero` (existing call sites bit-identical → golden vectors untouched) + `MatchSimulator` passing 30 s. **FIX WRITTEN AND VERIFIED NATIVELY** (g++ is available even though emsdk is not): `computeZero` gained a trailing `float max_time = 5.0f`; `MatchSimulator` passes **30 s**; the error message now names both numbers (`"trial flight did not reach 2310.000000 m within max_time=5.000000 s"`) instead of blaming the bullet. Compiled and run against the real engine sources — at `max_time=5` it throws at 2100/2500/3000 and passes at 1500/2000 (**old behaviour reproduced exactly**); at 30 s every range including 3000 m converges. Separately proved the 6-arg and 5-arg legacy calls still compile *and still get 5 s*, which is load-bearing: the golden vectors were generated through call sites that omit the argument. **`bindings.cpp` needed care** — embind ignores C++ default arguments and binds full arity, so binding the member pointer directly would have silently demanded a 7th argument from `engine-bridge/index.ts` and `validation/solve-driver.mjs`; it now binds an explicit 6-arg lambda (identical JS surface) plus a separate `computeZeroWithMaxTime`. **No JS change needed** — the fix is entirely inside the C++ `MatchSimulator`. 3 regression tests added to `tests/test_zeroing.cpp`. **OWNER TO DO:** rebuild the WASM (emsdk), then run `node GameBuild/validation/run.mjs` — it must still report **0.000e+0**, since nothing on the validated path changed. `bindings.cpp` and the gtest file are the only two pieces the agent could not compile (no emscripten headers, no gtest). **⚠️ THE FIX IS NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT — read this before calling ELR unblocked.** Timing the now-succeeding zero natively shows the solver **stops converging at long range and burns its full 1000-iteration ceiling**: 10.0 ms at 1500 m, **616.7 ms at 2000 m**, **1111.2 ms at 3000 m** (tol 1e-6, desktop g++ -O2 — WASM on an iPad will be slower). Loosening to 1e-5 helps at 2000/3000 but **not at 2500 m**, which is slow at *both* tolerances — so the tolerance is not the real problem. `computeZero` corrects each axis with an undamped `-atan2(error, -target.z)` step and oscillates once the launch angle gets steep. Net effect: the fix turns a **dead trigger** into a **~1 s freeze** on first engagement of a far station (cached per station by `simAt`, so once, not per shot). Logged in `Wiki/_gaps.md` as the real remaining ELR blocker; candidates are damped/secant iteration, a sane iteration ceiling, or moving the zero solve off the main thread. **Also found:** the same call explains the perf spike — the zero iterates to **1e-6 with a 1000-iteration ceiling**, costing 19.7 ms at 1900 m but **837 ms** at 1800 m and 2000 m. Erratic by convergence luck. `simAt` caches per station so it is a first-shot cost, but a station landing on a slow-converging range will stall hard. The bridge's own zero uses 1e-5 / 50 — worth aligning. |

Read: idle 17–18 **is** 60 fps against a 16.67 ms budget, so the empty 3 km scene is genuinely cheap and rendering throughput is *not* the problem — which is also why **adding trees would have been the wrong first move**: it changes the mean and buries the spike instead of isolating it. A near-double frame against a clean mean is a discrete **blocking** event; slow rendering raises the mean instead. One `setState` per shot, on a frame already spent, so measuring cannot inflate what it measures. **The ~22 ms on cursor movement is probably the test rig, not the game:** `onPointerMove` early-returns unless that pointer was captured by a `pointerdown`, so a hovering trackpad cursor runs **zero** of our JS — the cost is Safari hit-testing the absolutely-positioned HUD overlays and compositing a cursor that will not exist under touch. | P3-fix2 | **DONE (gates) — superseded by P3-fix3 above** | 2026-07-27 | (pending, owner-side) | **Target frames were sunk into the ground** (owner, on device: *"the farther targets are slightly below the ground… more obvious on these farther ones"*, and correctly noted it affected the flat build too). Cause: gongs are **constant-angular**, so their frames grow with distance — 2× the gong diameter, i.e. **6 m tall at 3000 m** — but every centre was pinned at a fixed 1.0 m. The frame's bottom edge is therefore `1.0 − frameH/2`, which goes negative past 1000 m and reaches **−2.0 m at the far station**. Exactly matches the reported gradient: fine near, worse far. Fixed with `targetCenterYFor(gongDiameter) = max(1.0, frameH/2 + 0.3)` — small near targets keep the standard height, big ones go on taller stands, and every frame now has 0.3 m of air under it. That is also what real ELR ranges do: a 3 m plate cannot have its centre a metre off the deck without being half-buried. Resulting centres: 1.00 / 1.30 / 1.80 / 2.30 / 2.80 / **3.30 m**. Occlusion margins re-verified unchanged (the fan dominates; the added elevation spread is under 0.06°). **4 tests reworked/added** — the two that encoded the old fixed-1.0 rule now assert the real invariant (*every frame clears the terrain directly beneath it*) rather than a magic number. Gates: vitest **695 passed / 52 files**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` green. |

On the slope that put **53 m of hill where the shooter stands**, burying the 11.7 m camera 41 m underground; the visible symptom was a thin band of terrain with the targets floating above it. Extracted to a named, tested `groundLocalYToDownrangeM()` precisely because this class of error is silent on flat ground. **(2) Target height was read at the LOS range but the target is PLACED at its ground run** — mildly circular on a slope, and it left every gong floating up to 0.14 m off the terrain. Now settled by three fixed-point passes (13 % max gradient ⇒ sub-millimetre residual; flat converges on pass one by construction). Also **widened the ground 300 → 1200 m**: 300 m across 3 km is a 1:10 ribbon whose side edges sit only ±2.9° off the sight line, well inside the scope's field — 1200 m pushes them past ±11° so they leave the picture. **5 new tests**: end-to-end mapping, monotonicity, an explicit "no hill where the shooter stands" regression that also asserts what the OLD formula produced, and per-station checks that every gong sits exactly `TARGET_CENTER_Y_M` above its own terrain in both variants. Gates: vitest **693 passed / 52 files**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` green. |

Replaced the closed-form flat solve with a ray-march against the profile in new pure **`scope/miss-projection.ts`** — marching, not solving, because a convex profile can be crossed at more than one radius and the FIRST crossing is the one that stops the bullet.
