### RESOLVED 2026-07-27 — pre-existing flaky wind test, seeded (owner-authorised)

`src/engine-bridge/wind-field.test.ts` → *"a freshly built preset, ensembled over time at a
fixed point, averages toward ≈0 (zero-mean turbulence)"* fails roughly **1 run in 6**.

**It is not caused by P0 or P1.** Evidence: it fails in isolation with only that file running;
nothing in the P0/P1 diff touches `wind-field.ts`; and the test never calls
`solveTrajectoryField` (the only function in that module that mirrors the code P0 changed) —
it uses `createWindField` / `advance` / `sample` only.

**Root cause.** `WindGenerator` draws its per-octave phase offsets from `btk::math::Random`
(`engine/src/physics/wind_generator.cpp:223–225`), and `bindings.cpp:326` states the game
leaves that RNG **clock-seeded**. So every `createWindField` builds a different field, and the
test's tolerance — `avgMag < 0.4 × meanAbsSample`, with a comment noting the empirical value
is "~0.2 … 0.4 leaves margin" — is not wide enough for unlucky draws. Observed failure:
0.682 vs a 0.593 bound, i.e. a ratio of 0.46 against the 0.40 limit.

**FIX APPLIED (owner-authorised 2026-07-27): seed the RNG, do not widen the tolerance.**
`beforeEach` in `wind-field.test.ts` now calls `module.Random.seed(1337)` — `beforeEach` and
not `beforeAll` because field construction consumes RNG, so a single seed would leave each
test dependent on how many fields ran before it (deterministic, but order-dependent — a
subtler version of the same bug). Verified **8/8 clean runs** in isolation, where the
unseeded version failed roughly 1 in 6, and the full suite is green at **649 tests**. The
game is unchanged: this seeds the TEST only. `Random::seed(uint32_t)`
is **already exposed in the bindings for exactly this purpose** — its comment says it exists
"so the app / validation harness can make the MatchSimulator's per-shot sampling reproducible",
and that binding an existing seed function "cannot alter any computed solve output". That makes
the test deterministic without weakening what it asserts, which is the distinction
`execution-protocol.md` §4.2/§4.3 draws.

Alternatives if the owner prefers: lengthen `DURATION_S` (more correlation times → tighter
ensemble, slower test), or run the assertion over several seeds and require the median to pass.
**Widening 0.4 is the one option to avoid** — it weakens the check to hide a determinism gap.

**OWNER NOTE (2026-07-27): "we'll have to circle back to that wind issue because it keeps
coming up."** Seeding fixes the test's determinism, not whatever keeps recurring in the wind
model itself. Logged here as a standing item rather than closed.

**Answered while planning, no work needed:** `range/steel-scene-api.ts` is a purely structural
interface with no `sceneType` gating, so the full plan's open item #3 (capability extraction,
its Stage 3) is a **no-op and can be dropped** — `ELRProbeScene` need only satisfy the same
shape `RangeScene` and `TestRangeScene` already do.

**Deferred observation (P1 will need it):** `ScopeView` derives `laneLenM` for wind-marker
filtering from a per-scene constant with an `else` fallback to `RANGE_A_GROUND.laneLengthM`. A
new scene type lands in that fallback. The probe sets `windMarkers: false` so it does not bite,
but the full ELR range must supply its own value or every flag past 500 yd is filtered out.

## Target system (owner-approved plan, `Design/archive/target-system-plan.md` — COMPLETE, archived 2026-07-31)

Origin: owner asked (2026-07-30) to build several reactive targets on the Test Range, on the
assumption that targets — like environmentals — are authored as their own files and imported
into scenes. **They are not:** every target today is a hand-written TS constant inside its own
range's config module (`TEST_RANGE_GONG`, `RANGE_A_RACKS`' distance-keyed tables, ELR's runtime
`solveLayout`), and `EnvironmentConfig` is likewise code rather than data. That correction
produced the plan: a **three-axis abstraction — Target × Mount × Group** — with target types as
typed TS modules, placements as JSON through a typed loader, zone-capable hit testing, and a
composable face layer stack for art/colour/drawn overlays. 16 tasks (T0–T10). Owner decisions
are listed in the plan's *Owner decisions* section; the batch ends with three targets on the
Test Range (hanging gong · stake IDPA · popper), all on the new system.

Two findings from the plan's verification pass are already load-bearing:
- **Mount is already an independent axis in shipped code** — `elr-range-config.ts` `mountFor()`
  returns `'stake' | 'rack' | 'panel'` per STATION, and `ELRRangeScene` derives the reaction
  from it (`swings: st.mount !== 'stake'`). Reaction mode therefore belongs to the mount.
- **A confirmed defect:** `ScopeView` overwrites a plate's *whole* atlas layer with the C++
  paint buffer, and ELR writes its bullseye rings through that same layer — so **the ELR rings
  are wiped on the first hit.** Owner authorized fixing it in this batch (T4b).

**BATCH COMPLETE 2026-07-31.** All 16 tasks (T0–T10) done; every on-device owner check
passed. The Test Range now carries three target types on three mounts — hanging gong
(chain-beam, swings) · stake IDPA (bolt-stake, bolted) · two poppers (hinge-stem,
knockdown, one group). Range A, the Wooded Zero Range and the ELR Range are unchanged
except for T4b's owner-approved ELR ring fix. Final gates: `ctest` **30/30** · golden
vectors **36 cases, worst rel diff 0.000e+0** · `tsc --noEmit` clean · vitest **1173
passed / 73 files** (from a 823/56 baseline) · `npm run build` green, 33 precache entries.
**Not built, deliberately** (see the plan's Deferred section): points/scoring math and any
zone HUD; the dueling tree / flip mode (T10 ships its seam); Texas star, plate rack,
runners, flappers; migrating Range A or ELR onto placement JSON; retiring
`bullseye-texture.ts` in favour of a `zones` face layer.

| Step | Status | Date | Commit | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **T10** | **DONE — full gate green** | 2026-07-31 | (pending, owner-side) | **The engine can now be told a pose it did not compute — the seam flipping targets need.** The ONLY task in the batch touching engine source, so it ran the full gate. `SteelTarget::setOrientation(q)` sets `orientation_` and re-derives `normal_`; one embind row; **8 new native tests**; `SteelReaction.setOrientation` on the TS side; `types.ts` gains the `Quaternion` ctor and the setter. **Why it was needed:** `hit()` picks which texture half to paint from `vel · normal_` and stores the impact at `inverse(orientation_) · local_pos` — both read the body's OWN state. T6's knockdown and the future flip mode compute their pose in TypeScript (the solver has no base hinge, angular limit, latch or reset actuator), so without this a flipped paddle would keep painting the half the player can no longer see, at the un-flipped position. **A refactor came with it, and it is the durable part:** `timeStep` had the orientation-normalise-then-recompute-normal pairing written out TWICE (the rotation integrator and the twist clamp). Both now call a private `syncNormalToOrientation`, which `setOrientation` shares — so there is ONE definition of that pairing and a future pose path cannot update the orientation while forgetting the normal, which is exactly the class of bug this task exists to fix. **The native tests pin both directions.** Un-flipped: default orientation is identity with the normal downrange; a downrange hit still paints the right half only; **setting the pose a target already has is a no-op** (a setter that perturbed on re-assert would be worse than none). Flipped: the normal reverses; an unnormalised quaternion is normalised rather than left scaling; a downrange hit paints the LEFT half and leaves the right alone; **the local position MIRRORS** (asserted via chip centroids, `u_unflipped + u_flipped ≈ 1`) because the impact is stored in the body's local frame; flipping back marks the other face while the FIRST mark survives — which is what a dueling-tree paddle requires; and velocity/angular velocity are untouched, because a caller driving the pose owns the motion. **T0's deliberate tripwire fired and was updated, not deleted:** it asserted `'setOrientation' in r === false` specifically so this task could not land silently. It now asserts the opposite plus the flip behaviour, and a sibling test re-pins the un-flipped case. A bridge test also round-trips a pose to catch the **argument-order trap**: the bridge passes `{x,y,z,w}` but the C++ constructor is `(w,x,y,z)`, and getting that wrong would silently rotate every flipped target somewhere else. **Mutation-checked four ways** — the setter storing the pose but leaving the normal stale (4 native tests fail), dropping the normalise (1 fails), the wrong quaternion arg order (1 bridge test fails), and never pushing the pose (3 fail). All four fail, sources restored, engine rebuilt clean. **Full gate:** `ctest` **30/30 passed** (was 22) · golden vectors **36 cases, worst rel diff 0.000e+0** — provably no ballistics path moved · WASM rebuilt with emcc 6.0.2 (245 747 → 247 898 B) · `tsc --noEmit` clean · vitest **1173 passed / 73 files** (was 1170) · `npm run build` green (33 precache entries). **This ships the SEAM, not the flip mode** — a dueling tree is a later task that consumes it. ~330 lines / 7 files. |
| **T9b-FIX2** | **COMPLETE — owner-confirmed on device 2026-07-31** | 2026-07-31 | (pending, owner-side) | **Target SELECTION was shape-blind, so a popper's head was unhittable.** Owner on device: *"on the poppers the bottom and middle circle accept hits and fall but the head area doesn't. Shots pass clean through them."* **The hit test was innocent** — `zoneAt` already returned `'plate'` for a head point, and a T8 test proved it. The failure was upstream, in `scope/aim-pick.ts`: `switchThresholdRad` sized the "the crosshair is ON this plate" test as a **circle of the plate's WIDTH** (`diameterM / 2`). Correct for every plate that existed before the target system, wrong for a 42″ popper that is 3.5× taller than wide — its head sits **11.7 mrad** above centre while 2× its angular *width* radius is only **6.7 mrad**. So aiming at the head never met the threshold, the auto-committed 100 yd gong kept the engagement, `resolveShot` resolved the shot on the GONG's plane, and the popper was never hit-tested at all. Hence "clean through": a shot really was fired, at another target. **Fix: `crosshairIsOnPlate`, an ELLIPTICAL test** that normalises the angular miss by the plate's own half-extent on each axis. `AimablePlate` gains `heightM?` (omitted ⇒ `diameterM`), which `PlateInstance` already carries, so both `ScopeView` call sites needed no change. **It reduces EXACTLY to the previous circular test for a round plate** — both denominators collapse to `switchThresholdRad` — and that equivalence is asserted over a grid of 5 diameters × 5 distances × 6 radii × 4 bearings rather than argued, so no shipped range can have moved. **The tempting wrong fix is explicitly guarded against:** widening BOTH axes to `max(halfW, halfH)` would make a popper selectable a metre off to the side and let it steal its neighbour's engagement; a test asserts a shot 0.7 m lateral is still NOT on it, and that mutation fails. Also asserted: a shot 2 m above the head is still rejected (taller is not unlimited — 2× half-height remains the limit), and `resolveTargetPlate` now hands the engagement to a popper aimed at its head while still leaving a committed gong alone when the crosshair is on neither. Ranking (`pickAimedPlate`, `angularMissRad`) deliberately still measures to plate CENTRES — that is what the 2026-07-27 ELR near/far bias fix turns on, and changing it was neither needed nor safe; only the "is it ON it" gate was wrong. The IDPA benefits automatically (its head was inside the circular threshold by luck, at 5.6 vs 6.7 mrad — closer than is comfortable). **Mutation-checked three ways** — ignoring height (reproduces the original bug), the wrong `max(w,h)` fix, and always-selectable — all three fail. That is cheap — base layers are written a handful of times at scene load — and the partial-upload optimisation is kept exactly where it matters, hit-time writes, by which point a frame has provably rendered. Tightened to assert the index ordering of every style-set against its draw call; both the stroke and fill variants now fail. (2) **Row 0 is the plate's BOTTOM**, not its top: `v = 0.5 + y` with y up and `texture.flipY = false` means buffer rows run bottom-to-top. Ops are emitted in that convention so tests assert against the real buffer layout, and `image` ops carry `flipY: true` to tell the rasteriser its bitmap needs flipping. Getting this backwards renders every silhouette upside down. Unstyled zones are simply not drawn. Sub-pixel strokes are clamped to 1 px — a stroke thinner than a texel vanishes. And a popper's fall is not emergent: a rod about its base, a hard stop, a mechanical reset. The decisive argument was testability — scene behaviour cannot be unit-tested here, so the one part that *can* be proven had to live where tests reach it. **Physics:** `θ̈ = (3g/2L)·sin θ` seeded from `ω₀ = 3·J·h/(m·L²)`, semi-implicit Euler, latch clamped at the configured angle, constant-rate rise (a reset motor is mechanical, not gravitational). Mass from the bounding box in steel (7850 kg/m³), which matches the `isOval: false` model and is **conservative** — an overstated mass falls more reluctantly, so a target never tips more easily than reality. **The invariant that matters is asserted directly: a harder hit falls FASTER but latches at the SAME angle** — fall speed belongs to the shot, resting angle to the hardware; if the latter varied per shot the plate would look like different geometry each time. Also pinned: a dead-centre hit at the hinge still topples (sin 0 = 0 is a fixed point, so the strike nudges off vertical — otherwise a real impulse leaves the plate standing forever); a down or rising target **ignores** further hits; a second hit on a still-toppling plate adds impulse; the out-of-play window covers the WHOLE down+rising span with no gap (the obvious bug being hittable again the instant it starts to rise, i.e. shootable while lying at 80°); and an end-to-end sanity case — a real 168 gr .308 at 600 m/s into a 3/8″ 12″×42″ popper topples it in well under a second. `hinge-stem`'s numbers are documented as hardware properties rather than taste (80° against a stop so accumulated marks stay visible; 2.5 s dwell; 60°/s reset ≈ 1.3 s). **A fragility found by a failing test and fixed in the SOURCE, not the test:** the controller derived the hinge from `plate.position` while reading the rest matrix from the mesh. Those agree in every real scene, but the rest matrix is what the rotation composes onto — so any other source silently rotates the plate about a point it is not attached to. Now both come from the rest matrix. My first fixture happened to disagree, which is how it surfaced; after fixing the fixture the mutation went **undetected**, so a dedicated test now makes the two disagree deliberately and asserts the rest matrix wins. **COMMIT resets ALL knockdowns**, not just the committed plate's group — the player is choosing what to shoot next, and leaving other steel face-down would quietly narrow the range. The `isStanding` filter sits in `fireSteel` **before** `resolveShot`, which keeps `game/shot.ts` free of reaction state and correctly removes a fallen plate from the aimed-plate pick as well as the hit test. `groupId` reset is tested with two racks: one stands up, the other stays down. **Mutation-checked nine ways** (latch overshoot, no nudge off vertical, re-knocking a down target, rising back in play, rise rate off by 100×, pivot from the wrong source, group reset ignoring the group, round plates losing the elliptical mass model, rotating about the centre instead of the hinge) — all nine now fail; the pivot-source one only after adding the test above. Round plates still construct with `isOval`/`heightM` **absent**, asserted, so the WASM integration tests are unchanged and green. That removes any chance of an aspect/scale mismatch at instancing time. The UV rule matches `plate-geometry.ts` exactly (`u = halfCentre + x·0.5`, `v = 0.5 + y/aspect`) because both generators feed the same C++ paint buffer through the same atlas — a divergence would render splats displaced from the impact on one shape only. Input guards reject <3 points, a non-positive aspect, and **clockwise winding** (which would invert every face and, on a FrontSide material, render an invisible plate). **`plate-surface.ts` gains `setBaseLayer` + `writeEngineLayer`**, the mechanism for the confirmed ELR ring-wipe defect (T4b applies it). The composite rule: a texel the engine left at the plate's paint colour is untouched ground so the art shows through; anything else is a chip and the engine wins. That works for arbitrarily complex art because the base is only consulted where the engine says no chip landed. **The safety guarantee is a test, not an argument:** with no base registered, `writeEngineLayer` is asserted **byte-identical** to `writeLayer` *including* the partial-upload bookkeeping — and neither Range A nor the ELR Range calls `setBaseLayer`, so both stay on the pre-T4 path exactly. Rewritten against the known emission order (caps first, then 6 vertices per outline edge), it now asserts perpendicular-to-edge, flat in z, and pointing outward `(ey, −ex)` for every one of the IDPA's 12 edges. **Mutation-checked six ways:** un-reversing the downrange cap (faces inward), dropping the `/aspect` from `v`, inverting rim winding, changing the rim's no-texture UV sentinel, reverting the composite to an overwrite (i.e. reintroducing the ELR wipe), and never consulting the base — each fails. A test asserts the head zone stays circular to 1e-12. Fixed with `pointInOutline` — inside OR within `NORMALISE_TOL` of the boundary — which also tolerates hand-traced art whose zone edges sit a fraction of a pixel out. **A test proves the tolerance is not slop** (a point 1e-2 outside still fails; 9e-4 passes, 1.1e-3 does not), because a containment check that accepts everything guards nothing. I split `target-geometry.ts` out of `target-type.ts` mid-task so each file has one job and T2/T3b/T4 import geometry without the type definitions — better structure, but it organises the lines rather than reducing them. **The registry is deliberately EMPTY**, with a test asserting length 0 so it cannot sit empty unnoticed while the zones-inside-outline loop passes vacuously; T7/T8/T9a each update that assertion. | **T0** | **DONE — gates green** | 2026-07-30 | (pending, owner-side) | **Characterization guards — the regression baseline for T1–T10.** Three new test files, no source changed. (1) `range/plate-geometry.golden.test.ts` — a whole-buffer FNV-1a signature over the disc's positions (`c3e48f19`) and UVs (`735c836d`), quantised to 1e-6 so it is platform-stable; the existing `plate-geometry.test.ts` covers the disc's *meaning*, this covers *any* vertex change, meaningful or not. Vertex count (480 = segments×12) and sentinel vertices are pinned alongside the hashes, because **a bare hash mismatch is undiagnosable on its own** — read those first when it goes red. Also pins the `chainRest[id*2 + side]` indexing contract: the reaction loop indexes it **unconditionally** on a hit, so a scene handing out non-contiguous `instanceId`s reads a stale matrix. `RangeScene` can't be constructed in the node env (its signs need a 2D canvas), so the contract is asserted against the real 10-rack/50-plate ladder rather than a live scene. (2) `game/firing-solution.hit-grid.test.ts` — `discHit` truth over a deterministic 249-point grid (13×13 lattice + 5 rings straddling the edge) across **every** real Range A diameter × three real bullet diameters, plus explicit boundary tests for the `<=` line-break convention and the bullet-radius term. This is what T2 will diff its zone-capable replacement against. (3) `engine-bridge/steel-target.paint.test.ts` — the **two-sided paint invariant** against the real WASM: a downrange hit chips only the shooter-facing (right) half and leaves the downrange half at clean paint, across repeat hits, with `repaint()` clearing both. That split is what makes a future flipping paddle work, and the test also **pins the current gap** by asserting `setOrientation` is absent from `SteelReaction` — so it fails deliberately when T10 lands. **Three corrections to my own plan text, found by running it:** Range A has **nine** distinct plate diameters, not eight (350 yd adds a 7″ plate — the plan said 2/3/4/5/6/8/10/12); hit counts *fall* as the plate grows, because the grid scales with plate radius while the bullet radius is absolute, so monotonicity only holds across bullets at a fixed plate; and an exact-boundary assertion has to be written at an origin-centred plate, since `centre.x + r` then `impact.x − centre.x` does not round-trip to exactly `r`. **Guards were mutation-checked, not just run green** — four deliberate source mutations (`<=`→`<`, dropping the bullet-radius term, perturbing the unit radius by 1e-4, swapping the shooter-cap UV half) each fail the new suites; all sources restored and re-verified. Gates: `tsc --noEmit` clean · vitest **844 passed / 59 files** (baseline was **823 / 56** — verified by excluding the three new files; note the plan and the ELR-RANGE row below both quote 844/57, which was the count *before* PROBE-DELETE removed 38 tests) · `npm run build` green (32 precache entries, unchanged) · **ctest + golden vectors N/A — no engine source modified.** |

## IDPA Hostage / No-Shoot Paddle Targets — owner defect round (2026-08-06)

Origin: the hostage assembly (windowed IDPA silhouette + two independently-shootable
paddles, on the Test Range at 60 yd) was already built; the owner reported four defects
on device, all on the CENTRE paddle / backing plate. Small and self-contained enough not
to need its own plan doc — logged here directly per the Working agreement. No engine
source touched, so `ctest` and `run.mjs` are **N/A** (recorded, not skipped).

The owner's four items, and what each turned out to be:

1. *"The first shot hits and it flips to a side correctly but then it is no longer able
   to be hit again. It's visible but it won't flip."* **Not a flip-state bug — the same
   geometry bug as (2).** `game/shot.ts` walks the rack in authored order and takes the
   FIRST plate whose zones the impact breaks; it has no depth or occlusion concept.
   `hostage-clamp-3way`'s ±0.15 m swing left the 6″ paddle entirely inside the 18″
   backing plate's outline at both swung stops, so every shot aimed at it resolved
   against the silhouette's `body-0`/`minus-1` instead. The REST stop worked only
   because the silhouette's `window` is an `isHole` zone that misses cleanly and falls
   through to the paddle. (The top paddle was unaffected: ±0.175 m already clears the
   much narrower head/neck outline — which is the control case that identified the
   cause.)
2. *"Its pivot point is too close to the edge of the circle so it only flips behind the
   main body rather than out to its side."* Fixed with (1): swing is now **±0.36 m**,
   derived rather than chosen — 0.2286 m (half the backing plate) + 0.0762 m (paddle
   radius) = 0.3048 m minimum clearance, plus 5.5 cm so it reads as swung out BESIDE the
   target. Derivation lives in `mount-registry.ts`'s `HOSTAGE_CLAMP_3WAY`.
3. *"The center hole should be a visible hole where the background can be seen and shots
   into the hole go through. Currently it's just a black spot."* Shots already went
   through (`isHole`); the FACE was a near-black `$void` zone fill. Added a real cut
   path: new `FaceLayer` kind **`cut`** (names `isHole` zones) → cut draw-ops carrying
   `cut: true` → an alpha-zeroing pass in `face-raster.ts` → the plate material carries
   the atlas alpha into `diffuseColor` and discards on **`alphaTest`**. Discard, not
   `transparent`, so no plate anywhere joins a sorted pass. `validateTargetType` now
   rejects a `cut` naming a zone that is not `isHole`, so the drawn opening and the
   scored hole cannot drift. `writeEngineLayer` keeps a cut texel cut, so a splat landing
   near the rim cannot scab the window shut.
4. *"Remove the artwork from this one (the cardboard brown and zones lines) main body
   should be just white and the paddles their current red is fine."* The hostage
   silhouette's face is now `fill` (white 0xffffff) + `cut` — no `image` layer, no
   `zones` layer. Zones still exist and still score, they are simply not drawn. The
   paddle colour is untouched.

Two deliberate non-changes: the shot loop's first-hit-wins ordering stays as is (making a
paddle hittable THROUGH the silhouette would be wrong physics — clearance is the right
fix), and the centre paddle keeps its render-only `zNudgeM`.

| Item | Status | Date | Commit | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H1 swing clearance | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | `hostage-clamp-3way` ±0.15 → **±0.36 m**; `steel-reactions.test.ts` cycle expectations updated; placement note rewritten with the cause. New `hostage-paddle.test.ts` block runs `resolveShot`'s own hit loop over the real shipped geometry at EVERY stop of both clamps — the regression that would have caught this. |
| H2 `cut` face layer | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | `target-type.ts` (kind + isHole validation) · `face-plan.ts` (cut ops, both faces, anisotropy-corrected) · `face-raster.ts` (`applyCuts`, byte-domain, bbox-bounded, runs AFTER the opacity pass) · `plate-surface.ts` (`PLATE_ALPHA_TEST`, alpha into `diffuseColor`, cache key → `plate-surface-v2`, cut texels survive engine writes). Cuts are computed analytically, NOT via canvas `destination-out`: which texels end up transparent is the one part that must be exact, and node has no canvas to check it with. |
| H3 white face + real window | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | `idpa-hostage.ts` face is now `fill`(white) + `cut(['window'])`; `IDPA_COLORS`/`VOID_HEX` no longer referenced; `IDPA_HOSTAGE_FACE_HEX` exported. Engine paint colour follows the fill, so splats composite against white. |
| **H5 FPS regression** | **DIAGNOSIS CONFIRMED — owner on device: "back up to 60"** | 2026-08-06 | | Owner on device: frame rate fell to **~10 FPS after the fix, on Range A too** — which loads no hostage target, so the target is ruled out and the shared `createPlateMaterial` is the suspect (the only per-FRAME change; everything else in H1–H3 is load-time or impact-time). Suspected mechanism: the `discard` that `alphaTest` inserts costs the draw its early-Z / hidden-surface-rejection fast path, worst on the tile-based iPad GPU. Added **`PLATE_FACE_CUTS_ENABLED`** in `plate-surface.ts`, **default `false`** — off restores the pre-fix material byte-for-byte (`alphaTest: 0`, no alpha term, no discard); the two branches carry different program cache keys so they cannot share a compiled shader. Cost while off: the window renders solid white, but shots STILL pass through it (`isHole` is hit-test-side, not shader-side). **If off restores the frame rate, the permanent fix is a triangulated hole in `plate-outline-geometry.ts`** — a real hole with no per-fragment cost. If it does NOT, the cause is elsewhere and needs a wider bisect. vitest 1463/86, `tsc` clean. **Owner confirmed on device: "It took a handful of reloads but it's now back up to 60"** — so the shared plate material was the cause and the hostage target was never implicated. The switch stays as the shipped state until H7 replaces it. |
| H6 swing dialed back | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | Owner on device (screenshot): ±0.36 m read as too far out. Now **±0.33 m** — 2.5 cm (~1″) above the 0.3048 m clearance floor, so what shrank is the margin, not the requirement. `hostage-paddle.test.ts`'s clearance test now asserts floor **+ 1 cm**: the owner tunes this by eye and the floor is invisible in a screenshot, and bare containment leaves the paddle's inner edge within one bullet radius of the silhouette, where the unhittable-paddle bug returns from the edge inward. Below ~0.315 m the honest fix is depth-aware hit testing (the paddle already sits 5 cm in front via `zNudgeM`, render-only today), not a smaller number. |
| H8 head paddle raised | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | Owner on device: "the shoulder target is overlapping the shoulder a bit causing some clipping." The paddle was centred on the head ZONE (1.586 m), but the 6″ disc's bottom edge reached **1.4 cm below the shoulder line** (1.5240 m — the 5 ft shoulder the backing plate's own `centreYM` derives from), and the two plates are coplanar, so the overlap z-fought. Owner chose to RAISE it over a z-nudge (asked, not assumed): `centreYM` 1.586 → **1.61 m**, bottom edge 1 cm clear. Above the shoulder the silhouette is only head/neck (half-width 0.0775 m) vs the paddle's 0.0988 m inner edge, so no overlap remains at either stop. Guarded by a `hostage-paddle.test.ts` assertion derived from `IDPA_SHOULDER_LOCAL_Y` — a 1 cm z-fight is invisible in a screenshot. |
| H9 flip actually FLIPS | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | Owner on device: "both flippers don't actually flip, they just slide to the side so the same face is always visible. If I shoot the right side of the flipper, it slides out to the side and the splat is still visible on the right side." Correct — `poseFlip` lerped X and never touched orientation. Now a **180° swing about the vertical axis midway between the two stops** (owner picked this over spin-in-place and horizontal tumble). One rotation delivers both asks: a half turn about the midpoint maps either stop exactly onto the other (so position stays single-sourced — no translation to drift out of step), and the far face comes round to the shooter, carrying the splat with it. Facing is an **accumulator** (`spunFromRad`/`spunToRad`), not derived from `state.index`: the 3-way cycle revisits `center` every other strike while the face does not. The arc is always toward the shooter (`sin(πt)` unsigned, only facing takes the travel's sign) — a door opening both ways would sweep the paddle through the backing plate on alternate strikes. `resetFlipTargets` now zeroes the accumulator too, since stop 0 + 2π looks identical but presents the wrong face. |
| H7 window as GEOMETRY | **DONE** | 2026-08-06 | | Closed by F2/F3 below, in the same pass as the flip-direction fix, on owner request ("still no hole showing, I'd like to take another crack at that"). The route planned here held up: `ShapeUtils.triangulateShape` always took a holes argument. |
| F1 flip direction REVERSED | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | Owner: "the action is actually backwards, the paddles shouldn't flip towards the shooter but away. Technically the paddle is behind the body target. The bullet goes through the center hole, hits the paddle, it flips away from the shooter." Correct, and it is momentum rather than taste — the bullet is travelling downrange. `poseFlip`'s arc term goes negative (`−|h|·sin(πt)`). The DEPTH follows from it rather than being a separate choice: `test-hostage-center`'s `zNudgeM` +0.05 → **−0.05**, because a paddle that swings downrange must hang BEHIND the plate or the arc sweeps it straight through. `RawPlacement.zNudgeM` is now documented as signed and load-bearing for a `flip` mount, not merely a z-fight nudge. Owner flagged this as a REUSABLE mount behaviour ("this flipper behavior will be used again a few times"), which is why the rule lives in `poseFlip` and only the depth is per-placement. |
| F2 holes moved off paint | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | The `cut` FaceLayer — one round old — is gone; holes are **`TargetType.holeZoneIds`**, sitting next to `shape` where geometry belongs. Validation still enforces the `isHole` agreement, so what you see through and what a bullet passes through cannot diverge. Deleted with it: `face-plan`'s cut ops, `face-raster`'s `applyCuts` and cut skip, `plate-surface`'s cut-texel guard, and the layer-ordering rule ("cut must be last") that only existed because paint layers composite. A net deletion. |
| F3 window is real GEOMETRY | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | `createPlateOutlineGeometry(outline, aspect, holes)` passes holes to `ShapeUtils.triangulateShape` (which always accepted them — the old call passed `[]`) and runs its rim loop over `[outline, ...holes]`, so a hole gets an inner WALL from the same code that gives the outline its outward rim. Correctness rides on WINDING and is enforced, not assumed: holes must be CW, which is both what earcut wants and what makes the wall face into the hole; a CCW ring throws rather than silently filling itself in and inverting. `holeRings` (target-geometry) polygonises a circular zone at 48 segments and normalises winding. Hole-wall verts carry rim UV (−1,−1) → bare steel inside the bore. `TestRangeScene` routes any type with holes through the outline generator, a disc included, so a holed disc cannot silently render solid. Output is byte-identical when no holes are passed — the guarantee that keeps the plain IDPA and the popper unmoved. |
| F4 alphaTest path deleted | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | `PLATE_FACE_CUTS_ENABLED`, `PLATE_ALPHA_TEST`, the alpha branch in the fragment patch and the dual cache key are all gone; `createPlateMaterial` is byte-for-byte the pre-regression version (cache key back to `plate-surface-v1`). NOT left as a dead switch — a kill switch nobody can flip is just a trap. The lesson survives as a ⚠️ on the function plus a test asserting `alphaTest === 0` and no `discard` in the patched shader, so the shader route cannot return by accident. Side effect worth knowing: the hostage silhouette no longer needs a face raster at all (its face is one flat `fill`), so it drops off `needsArt` — one fewer 512 KB atlas write at scene load. |
| F5 gates | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | vitest **1460 passed / 86 files** · `tsc --noEmit` clean · `vite build` green, 33 precache entries. `ctest` / `run.mjs` **N/A** (no engine source touched). Grep-confirmed that no live `alphaTest`/`discard` remains in the plate material — the only `discard` left in `src/` is `environment/clouds.ts`, pre-existing and unrelated. |
| F6 window shrunk to fit the paddle | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | Owner: "The hole should actually be a bit smaller than the paddle so no ring exists." The window was 70 spec-px = 0.0814 m against the paddle's 0.0762 m radius — the ~5 mm ring they saw. Now **62 px** (0.0721 m), so the paddle overlaps the rim by ~4 mm all round. The owner offered either knob ("whichever is easier") and the PADDLE is the wrong one: `HOSTAGE_PADDLE.defaultWidthM` is shared by both hostage mounts, so a 7″ paddle would push the head paddle's bottom edge back below the shoulder line (H8, fixed hours earlier) and squeeze the centre paddle's clearance floor from 0.3048 to 0.3175 m against a 0.33 m swing. Shrinking the window touches nothing else. Pinned by a test in `hostage-paddle.test.ts` — the window is authored in spec pixels and the paddle in metres, so neither file can see the relationship alone. |
| F7 splats land on the VISIBLE face | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | Owner: "The shoulder target only gets splats on the front side... When it flips to the other side, even after multiple shots, the back is always clean." Cause: `SteelTarget::recordImpact` picks the painted texture half from the body's own `normal_` and stores the impact at `inverse(orientation_) · (impact − position_)` — all read from the RIGID BODY, which a TS-driven pose never updates. So every splat went to the half that ends up facing downrange after a flip. **`setOrientation` already existed for exactly this** (engine, bridge, embind and paint tests all shipped with T10; its docstring even predicts this bug) — it was simply never called. Now pushed down before every flip strike, using `spunToRad` (the facing as it stands, since the strike precedes the new transition). Second, quieter bug fixed with it: there is NO `setPosition`, so the IMPACT is re-expressed in the frame the engine still believes it occupies (`enginePos`) — otherwise a paddle at a 0.33 m stop took every hit a third of a metre off a 15 cm face, i.e. clamped to the rim. Deliberately NOT extended to knockdown: a popper is filtered out of the rack unless standing (`isStanding`), so it can only be struck at the identity orientation the engine already assumes. Asserted both ways, plus that a chain-hung plate is never told (its `timeStep` owns its pose). |
| F8 gates | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | vitest **1466 passed / 86 files** · `tsc --noEmit` clean · `vite build` green. `ctest` / `run.mjs` **N/A** — `setOrientation` is called for the first time here but its C++ is untouched and already covered by `test_steel_target_setorientation.cpp`. |
| H4 gates | DONE | 2026-08-06 | | vitest **1460 passed / 86 files** (was 1437/86) · `tsc --noEmit` clean · `vite build` green, 162 modules, 33 precache entries. `ctest` / `run.mjs` **N/A** (no engine source touched). NOTE: `npm run build` was run with an out-of-tree `--outDir` because the sandbox cannot unlink the existing mounted `dist/` (`EPERM` on `prepare-out-dir`) — the compile itself is green; worth one native `npm run build` on the owner's machine to confirm the in-place write. |

## Dueling Tree (`Design/archive/dueling-tree-plan.md`)

> **CLOSED 2026-08-06** — all three tasks built, both owner-verification stops
> confirmed on device ("Looks great" / "Looks fantastic"), gates green
> throughout, committed and pushed. The plan doc is archived at
> `Design/archive/dueling-tree-plan.md`; `Design/feature-catalog.md` §F's
> "Steel target menagerie" entry updated to close it out.

Origin: a dueling tree on the Test Range — one 5-ft centre post carrying 5 round
paddles that all rest on one side; shooting a paddle swings it 180° around the
post to the other side, always arcing away from the shooter. Reuses the shipped
hostage-paddle flip mechanism (`flip.ts`, `poseFlip`) unchanged — this plan is
new GEOMETRY (a target type, two size-specific arm mounts, a post) arranged
around that existing reaction, not new reaction code. Three tasks (DT1/DT2/DT3),
two owner-verification stops (after DT2, after DT3); protocol-sized, checkpoint
after DT1 rolls straight into DT2.

| Task | Status | Date | Commit | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|

New **`range/targets/dueling-tree.test.ts`** (12 tests): type validates/is a one-zone disc/registered/accepts only the two tree-arm mounts; each mount's swing recomputed via `duelingTreeSwingM` rather than asserted against a literal; paddle-clears-post at both stops for both sizes; paddle stack fits the post (top edge ≤ post height, bottom edge > 0) for both sizes; pitch exceeds the largest paddle's diameter. `mount-type.test.ts`'s `listMountTypes()` roster and `target-type.test.ts`'s `listTargetTypes()` roster both updated (not deleted) to include the two new ids. **Gates:** vitest **1478 passed / 87 files** (was 1460/86) · `tsc --noEmit` clean · `vite build` green (163 modules, 33 precache entries). `ctest` / `run.mjs` **N/A** (no engine source touched). No `zNudgeM`, no `beamHeightM`. `_note` on the first entry carries the post centreline (−3.0), the arm derivation producing −3.1343, why 80 yd is exclusive to the tree, why the centres are 55/45/35/25/15″, and the DT3 8″ swap recipe verbatim. **`range/TestRangeScene.ts`**: new `case 'tree-post':` → `addTreePost(members, placement, mat)` — one `CylinderGeometry` at `DUELING_TREE_POST_RADIUS_M`/`_HEIGHT_M`, x recovered as `members[0].position.x + mount.flip.positions[1].xOffsetM / 2` (never a second hard-coded −3.0, same recovery `poseFlip` does for the swing pivot), built once per group; arms deliberately not drawn (a few px at 80 yd, logged below as deferred). **Also closed the `addFurniture` exhaustiveness gap flagged during plan review**: added an explicit `case 'pivot-post': break` (documents the hostage clamps' intentional no-op, previously an undocumented gap) and a `default: { const unhandled: never = placement.mount.furniture; throw ... }` arm — a future furniture kind with no case is now a `tsc` failure instead of a silent on-device no-render; ran clean against every currently-registered furniture kind, so nothing existing was already unhandled. **`range/targets/dueling-tree.test.ts`** (+17 tests, 29 total): placement-level invariants over the REAL shipped rows via `getTargetPlacements('test-range')` — all five resolve sharing one groupId/distance/mount; `centreYM` matches `duelingTreePaddleYM(0..4)` in authored order; `xOffsetM` matches `postX(−3.0) − swing/2`; **first-hit loop** (mirroring `hostage-paddle.test.ts`'s pattern) resolves a shot at paddle *i* to paddle *i* — not a neighbour — at BOTH of its stops, for all five, run against `hitTargetZone` the same way `game/shot.ts` actually resolves a hit; no dueling-tree placement carries a non-zero `zNudgeM`. Three pre-existing roster/count assertions elsewhere updated (not deleted) for the new 12-entry Test Range: `test-range-targets.test.ts` ("seven authored targets"→twelve, chain-slot count 7→12, target-type-id set gained `dueling-tree-paddle`), `placements.test.ts`'s shipped-file id-list assertion. **Gates:** vitest **1483 passed / 87 files** (was 1478/87) · `tsc --noEmit` clean · `vite build` green (163 modules, 33 precache entries). `ctest` / `run.mjs` **N/A** (no engine source touched). Hit the same mounted-`dist/`-`EPERM`-on-unlink sandbox artifact as DT1/H4 on every build attempt this task (confirmed it recurs on ANY existing `dist/`, not just a specific stale one — rename always succeeds where unlink doesn't); worked around each time by renaming the existing `dist/` aside before building. ## Dial Moves the View (`Design/Plans/dial-moves-view-plan.md`)

Origin: turning a turret changed a number and nothing else — the sight picture
never moved, so the player never saw which way "up" goes. After this plan the
turrets move the sight picture in real time, as on a rifle held in a vise: dial
elevation UP and the crosshair walks DOWN the target. The shot math is untouched
— rotating the sight line by the dial changes `resolveShot`'s `aimError` by
exactly minus the dial, so `applied = aimError + dial + playerZero` is unchanged
and **dialing alone still cannot move an impact**; re-aiming is what does. Three
tasks (DV1/DV2/DV3), one owner-verification stop (after DV2); checkpoint after
DV1 rolls straight into DV2.

| Task | Status | Date | Commit | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DV1 pure turret-view module + tests | DONE — checkpoint | 2026-08-06 | — (rolls into DV2) | New **`scope/turret-view.ts`** (pure — no THREE, no DOM, no React, matching `scope/scope-projection.ts`): `SightOffset`, `SIGHT_GLIDE_TAU_S` (0.027 s ⇒ ~3τ ≈ 80 ms, owner decision 1), `SIGHT_SNAP_EPS_RAD` (1e-9, far below a 0.1-mil click), `sightOffset(scope, playerZero?)` = live turret + stored zero (`playerZero` included so Confirm Zero does not jump the view — §3.1; the hidden `zeroOffsetRad` is **never** read here, §3.3/protocol §4.8), `sightAimAngles(holdPitch, holdYaw, offset)` — pitch **ADDS** elevation, yaw **SUBTRACTS** windage (the camera convention is asymmetric: +pitch looks down, +yaw looks right), `sightLineDir(pitch, yaw)` = the closed form of `Euler(−p, −y, 0, 'YXZ')` applied to `(0,0,−1)`, and `easeSightOffset(cur, target, dt, tau?)` (`k = 1 − exp(−dt/τ)`, snap inside eps). New **`scope/turret-view.test.ts`** (27 tests, 6 groups): offset sums both terms / dial-alone when no stored zero; the §2.2 direction table asserted in the player's words ("dial elevation UP → sight line pitches DOWN (crosshair walks down; target rises)") incl. the owner's 1-MOA-high-and-left case; **THREE parity** over 7 `(pitch, yaw)` pairs to 1e-12 so the pure module and `ScopeView` cannot drift; **THE INVARIANT** — 6 dial combinations up to ±3 MIL through the real `resolveShot` all land within 1e-4 m of the no-dial impact (actual residual ~1e-7 m, from `atan2` of a plane intersection vs a plain angle — first-order-exact, expected, not a bug); the positive half — re-aiming after 1 MIL up/right moves the impact by `1 mrad × distance` on that axis and leaves the other axis put; and the glide's own contract (reaches ~95% in 3τ, frame-rate independent — one 20 ms step ≈ twenty 1 ms steps to 1e-12, snaps exactly onto target, holds still when settled, honours a tuned τ). **Sign convention mutation-tested:** temporarily flipping `sightAimAngles` to `−elev / +wind` fails 12 of the 27 tests including the whole invariant group, so the tests genuinely pin the plan's most-likely-to-be-inverted line; reverted immediately. No production code wired yet (DV2 does that), so behaviour is unchanged at this checkpoint. **Gates:** vitest **1514 passed / 88 files** (was 1487/87) · `tsc --noEmit` clean · `vite build` green (33 precache entries, 1605.64 KiB). `ctest` / `node GameBuild/validation/run.mjs` **N/A** — no engine source touched anywhere in this plan. The mounted-`dist/`-`EPERM`-on-unlink sandbox artifact (DT1/DT2/DT3/H4's known class) did **not** recur this task; the build recreated `dist/` cleanly. |
| DV2 wire it into the scope view | **AWAITING OWNER** — on-device verification | 2026-08-06 | not yet run (owner-side) — message in session | **`scope/ScopeView.tsx`** only (+52/−1, no new files): (1) one import from `./turret-view`; (2) new `sightOffsetTarget()` beside `steelGearCtx` — reads `session.scope` + the active rifle's `playerZero` off the store and nothing else, deliberately **not** `steelGearCtx()` (which resolves specs and builds a whole solve context — no business running every frame); (3) `st` gained `sight`, **seeded** with `sightOffsetTarget()` at scene setup rather than glided from zero, so entering a range with a zeroed rifle or dope still on the turret is settled on the first frame (trap §4.2); (4) one line in the frame loop, immediately *before* `camera.quaternion.copy(...)`, easing `st.sight` toward the target on the loop's already-clamped `dt` (50 ms cap, so a stalled frame cannot overshoot); (5) `aimQuaternion` adds `+ st.sight.elevRad` to the pitch term and `− st.sight.windRad` to the yaw term, with the §2.2 direction table copied in above it as a comment. **Verified no bypass path:** the file contains exactly one `setFromEuler` (the edited one) and all five sight-line consumers — `findAimed`, `findAimedTarget`, `commitRef`, both FIRE paths, the mirage aim-distance dispatch — go through `aimQuaternion`, or through `camera.quaternion` *after* it is copied from `aimQuaternion` in the same frame (line 1779), so every one of them resolves against the exact eased sight line drawn on screen and picks the feature up for free. No call-site changes, no save-schema change (the offset is derived every frame from already-persisted state — no `schemaVersion` bump, no migration), no new dependency, and `zeroOffsetRad` appears nowhere in the diff. **Gates:** vitest **1514 passed / 88 files** · `tsc --noEmit` clean · `vite build` green (33 precache entries, 1606.11 KiB). `ctest` / `run.mjs` **N/A** — no engine source touched. Sandbox `dist/`-`EPERM` artifact did not recur. ## Resolved escalations

### 2026-07-13 — RESOLVED: npm `UNABLE_TO_GET_ISSUER_CERT_LOCALLY`

**Fix applied:** `npm config set cafile /Users/analyst/node-ca.pem` (writes to the
user-level `~/.npmrc`; **not** committed to this repo, and reversible with
`npm config delete cafile`). Verified: `npm ping` → PONG, `npm view react version`
→ 19.2.7, `npm view vite version` → 8.1.4. `npm install` will now work.

**Why it works / true root cause:** the ambient `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` points at
`/Users/analyst/.claude/apple/certs/bundle.pem`, which contains only **11 legacy
Apple/GeoTrust/VeriSign/Comodo roots and does NOT include `DigiCert Global Root G2`**
— the root that anchors `npm.apple.com`'s real chain. Node's *built-in* store does
include that root, but in this Node v26 build, having `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` set
effectively caused Node to trust only that incomplete bundle for npm's connections
(so chain-building failed). Pointing npm's own `cafile` at the pre-existing complete
CA file `~/node-ca.pem` (a 180-cert keychain dump already on the machine from another
project) gives npm a self-sufficient trust set that overrides the broken ambient one.
This is a legitimate npm configuration using valid CA certs — not a security bypass,
no env-var or sandbox change, no domain-allowlist change.

**To reproduce the fix from scratch** (if `~/node-ca.pem` is ever missing): a dump of
Node's built-in roots suffices — `node -e "const t=require('tls'),f=require('fs');f.writeFileSync('/some/ca.pem',t.rootCertificates.join('\n'))"`
then `npm config set cafile /some/ca.pem`.

### 2026-07-31 — RESOLVED: CI `ENOENT` on `idpa.test.ts`/`popper.test.ts` (spec files were git-ignored)

**Symptom (owner-reported, GitHub Actions):** `idpa.test.ts` failed with `ENOENT: no such
file or directory, open '.../Documentation/Targets/idpa-target.svg'`. `popper.test.ts` has
the identical bug (same pattern, not yet surfaced in the pasted log — likely the same run,
truncated) — both tests' `SPEC_PATH` read straight out of `Documentation/`.

**True root cause:** `Documentation/` is entirely git-ignored (`.gitignore`: "Ground-truth
sources & synthesized notes are kept local-only, never pushed") — intended for the
copyrighted Litz/McCoy/FM 23-10 PDFs, but the IDPA target-system work (`4ae0bbe`, closed same
day as the bc-truing plan, 2026-07-31) filed its two **original, non-copyrighted** SVG specs
(`idpa-target.svg`, `idpa-popper.svg` — hand-authored geometry, not third-party source
material) in that same folder. The "spec sync" tests that guard `idpa.ts`/`popper.ts`'s
embedded constants against those SVGs worked locally (the folder exists on the owner's Mac)
but could never pass on a clean GitHub Actions checkout, which only has what's pushed.

**Fix:** added a tracked mirror — `GameBuild/app/src/range/targets/spec/{idpa-target.svg,
idpa-popper.svg}` — byte-diffed identical to the `Documentation/Targets/` originals and to
the existing tracked `public/targets/idpa-target.svg` (confirmed with `diff`, all three
match). Repointed both tests' `SPEC_PATH` at the local `./spec/...` copy (no more `../../../../../Documentation/...`
traversal); updated the provenance comments in `idpa.ts`/`popper.ts` to name the tracked
mirror alongside the owner's original authoring location. `Documentation/Targets/` originals
left untouched (still git-ignored, still where the owner would hand-edit the art) — if that
source ever changes, `spec/` needs a manual re-copy (same "keep it in sync by hand" burden
`public/targets/idpa-target.svg` already carried; noted in the test file comments so it isn't
lost).

**Verified:** temporarily renamed the local `Documentation/` folder out of the way (`mv
Documentation Documentation.hidden-for-ci-test`) to reproduce the CI checkout exactly —
`idpa.test.ts` + `popper.test.ts` **44/44 green** with `Documentation/` fully absent; restored
the folder after. Full suite re-run with `Documentation/` back: `npx vitest run` **75 files /
1197 tests green** (unchanged count — no tests added/removed, only a path fixed); `npx tsc
--noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean (scratch-outDir verification, same sandbox `dist/`
permission caveat as the bc-truing session, unrelated to this fix). Confirmed the two new
SVGs are NOT caught by any `.gitignore` rule (`git check-ignore` exits 1 for both) and show as
untracked (`??`), ready for the owner to `git add`.

**Note for the harness maintainer (not blocking us):** the underlying bug is that
`/Users/analyst/.claude/apple/certs/bundle.pem` is an incomplete trust set that, when
loaded via `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, breaks Node TLS for hosts anchored by roots it
omits (e.g. DigiCert Global Root G2). Regenerating that bundle to be complete would
fix this environment-wide for all Node tools without the per-tool `cafile` workaround.

## Deferred observations
- (wind-system-btk-port W1/P11, found 2026-07-31 answering an owner question)
  **4 of the ELR range's 6 wind markers are tree-occluded from both firing
  points.** Verified with a throwaway vitest scratch script (deleted after,
  same discipline as the W1 offset re-solve) that ran the ACTUAL seeded tree
  field + `solveLayout`'s per-firing-point cull sets through
  `occludingTreeIndices` against each marker's pole-top position: 250/500/750/
  1000 m are all blocked by at least one tree from BOTH low and high; only
  1500/2000 m are clear. Root cause: the W1 offset solve (P11) only checked
  that markers don't block a farther STATION's gong bearing and don't collide
  with rack/tree geometry at their own spot — nothing checked whether trees
  block the sight line TO the markers themselves, which matters on a real
  wooded hillside (not a mown lane). **Owner asked to hold off — fix later,**
  not part of this plan's W2–W7 scope. When picked up: likely needs a real
  sight-clearance solve per marker (mirroring `chooseOffset`/`solveLayout`'s
  approach for stations) rather than one constant lateral offset, since trees
  are irregular and a single offset that clears one distance won't
  necessarily clear another.
- (D16) **The two zero DRAW keys are now misnomers.** `RifleInstance.draws.zeroH`
  / `.zeroV` are persisted normalized [0,1] values; under D16 they mean MAGNITUDE
  and DIRECTION, not horizontal and vertical. Renaming them to
  `zeroMagnitude`/`zeroDirection` is a save-schema change (guardrail §6: version
  bump + migration + fixture corpus), so it was deliberately NOT done as part of
  the offset change. `deriveRifleTruth` documents the reinterpretation at the
  point of use.
- (D16) **Existing saves with a confirmed zero are now mis-zeroed.** A stored
  `playerZero` was cancelling a ~1 MOA offset; the same rifle now carries 5-35
  MOA, and the stored correction no longer matches. There is no sensible
  migration (the truth genuinely changed), so affected rifles simply need
  re-zeroing — which is the new intended flow anyway. Flagged rather than
  silently shipped.
- (D16) **Consequence 1 of the plan is NOT implemented.** The DOPE book's "not
  zeroed" / "not chronoed" status notes need the Data Book UI (task 2.4f), which
  does not exist yet. The offset and the visible-miss handling landed; the
  player-facing explanation of why an unzeroed table is provisional did not.
- (docs) **Rows for Increment 1 reference plan docs that no longer exist anywhere.**
  `increment-1.5-plan.md` … `increment-1.8-plan.md` were deliberately DELETED when
  Increment 1 closed (see `CLAUDE.md`: "Per-task plan docs for Increments 0-1 were
  removed once closed (history in PROGRESS.md + git tags)"), so those references
  are dangling by design, not by accident. Left as written rather than scrubbed —
  the rows are a historical record of what was done at the time, and rewriting
  them to hide a pointer would be editing history to look tidier than it was. The
  archived increment-2.x plans, which DO still exist, were repointed to
  `Design/archive/` on 2026-07-26.
- (not ours) **`engine-bridge/wind-field.test.ts` is intermittently flaky.** The
  zero-mean-turbulence assertion failed **once** during Stage 5 verification, in a
  file Stage 5 does not touch. Investigated rather than ignored: it then passed
  6/6 in isolation and 3/3 in full-suite runs, and nothing in the change shares
  state with `wind-field.ts`. The test's own comment says the margin is
  empirical ("~0.2 for 'Moderate'... 0.4 leaves margin"), so a preset whose phases
  land unluckily could plausibly cross it. NOT re-tuned — loosening a tolerance to
  silence a check is exactly what execution-protocol §4.2/§6 forbid, and one
  failure is not enough evidence to justify changing a threshold that documents a
  physical claim. Logged so that if it recurs there is a prior sighting to point
  at, with a real fix (seed the preset explicitly, or ensemble over more
  correlation times) rather than a wider bound.
- (wooded-zero/2b) **The bullet trace is invisible against the white target
  boards.** Investigated as a suspected bug on owner report 2026-07-26; it is not
  one — the trace fires and flies correctly, it just cannot be seen. Cause is
  inherent: `BulletTrace` uses AdditiveBlending in warm copper (1.0, 0.75, 0.45),
  and additive over a near-white board (`#f2efe6`) is a no-op. Owner called it
  fine and it never mattered before because Range A's steel is dark and the old
  Zero Range's paper was small against grass — the new bay is the first place
  with a large bright surface right where the tracer terminates. If it ever wants
  fixing, the options are a dark outline pass on the trace, a non-additive
  fallback material when the terminal surface is bright, or simply toning the
  board face down from white. Not worth doing speculatively.
- (wooded-zero/2a) ~~Paper bays get no live wind field~~ **RESOLVED in 2b, and it
  turned out not to be a conflict.** The §7.3 decision is a very light *steady*
  breeze with gust scale 0 — which is exactly what the two `isSightIn` branches
  already provide (dialled mean, no gust, markers and shot agree). So the wooded
  bay keeps that path and simply enters with a non-zero mean
  (`WOODED_ZERO_WIND_MPS = 1.1` at 90°, i.e. full-value) instead of the sight-in
  bay's `setWind(0)`. No per-bay wind plumbing needed. Stage 5 reads the same
  field for canopy movement, so vegetation and bullet stay consistent by
  construction.
- (wooded-zero/2a) ~~`sight-in-marks.ts` / `sight-in-target-texture.ts` should be
  renamed~~ **DONE 2026-07-26** (owner: "rename what needs to be done to keep
  things clear"). Now `paper-target-marks.ts` / `paper-target-texture.ts`;
  `rasterizeSightInArt` → `rasterizeZeroingArt`. Only the two genuinely shared
  modules moved — `SightInScene.ts` and `sight-in-config.ts` are correctly named
  already. The rename also surfaced two stale comments worth more than the rename
  itself: `WoodedZeroScene`'s header still claimed "boards scale with distance to
  a constant angular size" (reversed on device three rounds earlier), and
  `SightInScene` still exported a `SightInTargetInstance` alias that nothing
  imported. Both fixed. SVG assets deliberately untouched (precache manifest).
- (2.3c2 → RESOLVED in 2.3d) Sight-in shot scatter was {0,0} (wobble-only group); 2.3d added
  `createGearScatter` so the sight-in group now reflects the gear's TRUE inherent-precision + MV/BC
  dispersion (match tighter than bulk). The gear-driven CENTER was already correct in 2.3c2.
- (1.5d) **Impact-sound energy scaling uses a FIXED reference** (`REFERENCE_IMPACT_ENERGY_J
  = 2500` in `audio/audio-model.ts`), deliberately absolute (not per-load) so a
  future weak round (e.g. a .22) rings quieter than a .308 — the cross-cartridge
  effect the owner asked about. The current 6.5 CM sits near 1× across Range A. It's
  a tuning knob; revisit when more cartridges exist. The **muzzle report volume is
  fixed at 1.0** (not energy-scaled) since impact energy is range-dependent while
  the muzzle blast is not; per-cartridge report loudness can come with multi-load.
- (1.5d) **A miss makes NO impact sound** (owner 2026-07-14, supersedes plan D3's
  ricochet-for-miss): a bullet into dirt/berm doesn't ring or ricochet, so only a
  hit on steel plays a ping. The ricochet clip was removed entirely. If a real
  dirt-impact "thump" sample is ever added, a soft miss cue could return — but by
  default misses are silent (just the muzzle report).
- (1.5d) **Ambient background loop deferred** (plan D3): BTK's `background_noise.mp3`
  is 3.9 MB against the 8 MiB precache budget; only the 3 small one-shots ship now.
  Add/transcode the ambient bed in 1.8 polish if wanted.
- (1.5a) **Steel swing duration is long (~15 s to fully settle)** — it falls out
  of the C++ SteelTarget's damping/chain constants (`LINEAR/ANGULAR_DAMPING` 0.5/s,
  stiff chains), identical to BTK steel-sim, so it "matches side-by-side." If the
  owner finds hung plates keep swinging too long for gameplay, tune via the
  reaction (e.g. a settle-time cap or a damping override in the bridge) rather than
  editing the oracle. Not touched in 1.5a (faithful to steel-sim first).
- (1.5a) **The SteelTarget texture/paint-removal path is intentionally unused**
  (plan D2): the plate is a shared InstancedMesh, incompatible with per-target
  textures, so the reaction constructs the target with a tiny 8px texture and the
  game will draw its own impact marks in **1.5c**. `getTexture`/`drawImpactOnTexture`
  are never called from the app.
- (1.5a) **One C++ SteelTarget per *struck* plate, created lazily and deleted on
  settle** — never one-per-plate. Concurrency is bounded by how many plates are
  swinging at once (a handful). The pose mirror reads the plate's *rest* instance
  matrix (scale + face-shooter rotation) at strike time, so it's robust to Range A
  config retunes; only the struck instance's matrix is rewritten (instancing intact).
- (1.5a) **Impact velocity for the impulse is the shooter→impact ray at the load's
  remaining speed** (from the 1.4 solve's `velocityMps`), not the true curved
  terminal velocity vector. The downrange component dominates, so the impulse
  direction/magnitude are faithful for the reaction; exact terminal-velocity
  vectoring (drop/spin components) can be revisited if a hit ever looks wrong.
- (1.5c) **No persistent impact mark — dust puffs only.** The first 1.5c build
  placed a persistent sprite "scuff" at the impact point; on a swinging plate it
  read wrong (world-anchored, didn't ride the swing), and the paired GPU dust
  shader didn't render. Owner 2026-07-14: dropped the mark; a shot's hit/miss
  feedback is now carried entirely by a transient (~0.9 s) sprite dust puff,
  colour-keyed by outcome. If lasting on-plate marks are wanted later, they'd
  need to be parented to the live plate pose (per-plate mark group transformed
  with the reaction quaternion) — deferred.
- (1.3b) **The 0.9 feel model (wobble/breath/recoil) is now duplicated** in
  `spike/AimSpike.tsx` (legacy spike) and `scope/ScopeView.tsx` (the real
  pipeline). ScopeView's copy is now the canonical one; AimSpike is a throwaway
  that can be deleted once the scope loop is fully established (or the shared
  bits extracted to a `scope/aim-model.ts` if a third caller appears). Kept
  duplicated deliberately to avoid touching the signed-off spike.
- (1.3b) **ScopeView does not yet consume dial state or model sight-height/
  ballistics.** The reticle is centred and the shot just recoils; dialed
  elevation/windage moving the reticle (holds) and real impact arrive with the
  firing-solution plumbing (1.4) and the loop/HUD (1.6). Reticle minor step is
  1 mil / 1 MOA with labelled majors every 5 — easy to retune (0.9-style) if the
  owner wants finer hashes for ranging. **REVISION 2026-07-14 (owner: MOA was
  "a bit noisy"):** MOA reticle cadence made variable via cadence "bands" —
  **1-MOA hashes to 15, every 5 to 50, every 10 beyond** (MIL unchanged: every 1,
  major/label every 5). Majors (longer + labelled) remain multiples of 5. Verified
  HERE: tsc clean, vitest 61/61 (+2 MOA-cadence tests), build green. Committed.
- (1.2) **Range A uses BTK's authored-inputs model (rev 4, owner 2026-07-14):**
  three independent authored inputs per rack — fixed frame width
  (`RACK_WIDTH_YARDS`, BTK's 1.5→3 yd ladder), an explicit plate list
  (`PLATE_INCHES`, 5 plates/rack, physical inch sizes, 2″ floor), and
  distance/offset — with **only the catch berm derived** from the frame
  (base = rackWidth×2.2, height = rackHeight×1.1, ported from steel-sim). Plates
  hang at 0.5× beam height. `PlateSpec.moa` is derived metadata (HUD/teaching).
  Difficulty scales with range naturally (big MOA near → sub-MOA far). Easy to
  retune the plate lists once the scope pipeline (1.3) and shot loop (1.4/1.6)
  show what plays well. Round plates only (no ovals). Fan (`X_OFFSET_YARDS`) is
  solver-guarded by the occlusion test — retune sizing ⇒ re-solve.
- (1.2) **`RangeScene.plates[]` already carries per-plate world metadata**
  (rackId, distanceM/Yards, diameterM, world position, InstancedMesh instanceId)
  — the seam the 1.4/1.5 hit-sim + reactive-steel will consume. Nothing reads it
  yet. Plate hit-testing/reaction is NOT in 1.2 (that's 1.4/1.5).
- (1.2) **RangeView input is a placeholder look-around** (drag pan + zoom
  slider), deliberately minimal to inspect the scene. It is NOT the scope
  pipeline; task 1.3 replaces it with the real second render pass + reticle and
  reuses the task-0.9 aim/wobble/breath-hold model. No sight-height/ballistics.
- (1.1) **`settings.sensitivity` is store-only, NOT persisted.** Save schema v1
  (`persistence/schema.ts`) has only `unitsPrimary`; adding `sensitivity` to the save
  requires a `schemaVersion` bump + migration + fixture (guardrail §4.6), and the
  schema.ts note reserves **v2 for Increment 2** (rifles/lots/DOPE). So 1.1 persists
  only `unitsPrimary`; `sensitivity` resets to its default (1.0) on reload. Fold
  `sensitivity` into the Increment-2 v2 bump. `persist-settings.ts` documents this;
  a test asserts the intentional omission.
- (1.1) Store **not yet wired into `App.tsx`** (no scene/UI to drive it until 1.2/1.6).
  App-shell hydration = `loadSettingsInto` on start + `persistSettingsOnChange`
  subscription; hook them up when the Range A UI lands.
- (0.9, owner requirement) **Hold wobble must be a user-adjustable setting in the
  real game** (amplitude control + off). Candidate future tie-in: position/support
  quality (catalog §5 human factors, currently deferred) could drive the default.
  Recoil behavior likely also becomes per-rifle (magnums kick harder) — Increment 1.4+.
- (0.9, owner idea → likely core mechanic) **Breath-hold/respiratory-pause button**:
  steadies the hold on a limited air budget with oxygen-debt penalty for overholding.
  Ships in the spike (iter 4); should carry into the real scope pipeline (task 1.3+)
  and pairs naturally with the trigger-breathing-recoil Wiki article (§5, unwritten —
  a future demand-driven article could cite the real technique this teaches).
- (0.4d) **Sight height over bore is not modeled** — the bridge zeroes the bore
  line through the target, so debug-table drops are bore-line values, not scope
  come-ups. Must be added (engine takes it via initial Y position, or bridge-side
  offset) before the task-1.6 DOPE table is real. The screen carries a visible
  footnote.
- (0.4d) `validation/match-check.mjs` intentionally duplicates the bridge's solve
  sequence (Vite-free oracle-harness seed). If the bridge solve changes, keep them
  in sync by hand — task 0.7 should consider extracting a shared driver.
- (0.4d) The owner-review sandbox (Linux) cannot run the Mac-native toolchain
  (typescript 7 / esbuild platform binaries) — only Node+WASM checks are portable
  across the two agent environments. Plan Mac-side verification for any task
  finished from that side.
- (0.4d) Stale `.git/index.lock` had to be cleared via the sandbox delete
  permission; the review sandbox cannot delete under `.git/` by default.

## Blocked / escalations
- (none — all Increment 0 tooling in place as of 2026-07-13)

## Owner decisions log
- 2026-07-31: **Execution protocol amended — plan-declared pause points replace the
  per-task owner stop; the ~400-line limit becomes planning guidance.** Owner: work is
  almost always plan-driven, so the agent authoring a plan now decides where the pause
  points fall — **checkpoints** (regression / new-feature testing that needs no owner
  input; run them and keep going) and **owner-verification stops** (halt and wait).
  Hard requirement on the latter: *"even if there's no UI changes, there should be
  something in a dev panel or logging that I am able to test and confirm."* The
  ~400 changed lines / ~10 files figure is no longer a mid-task hard stop — it is
  guidance for splitting a plan into tasks; a task that runs over finishes if it is
  still one coherent unit of work, with the overrun noted here. Written into
  `execution-protocol.md` (new §2b; §2.8, §3, §5 amended) and `CLAUDE.md` (Working
  agreement).
- 2026-07-31: **Git is owner-run; plans now declare commit points.** Owner: *"I'll
  need to handle all the git commands myself, [but] I would like the plan to include
  key points that should be committed and pushed and include the note that should be
  used at the time. I've been doing comments myself but they have no pattern."* The
  agent never writes git history (read-only `status`/`diff`/`log` is fine). Plans mark
  each task `commit` / `commit + push` / `—` and carry the ready-to-paste message
  verbatim, repeated in-session on arrival. Default placement: every
  owner-verification stop, before anything hard to unwind (wide refactor, rename
  sweep, schema migration, engine change), after work that would hurt to redo, and at
  plan completion. Never offered on a red gate. Message pattern:
  `<plan-slug> <task>: <imperative summary ≤72 chars>` + 1–3 what-changed bullets.
  **No gate/test results in commit messages** (owner, same session: *"unless test
  verification is helpful to you, I'd say ditch it from the git comments"*) — a commit
  point already requires green gates, so restating that is noise; the real numbers are
  logged per task here in PROGRESS.md. The old `inc<N>/task<M>:` convention is
  retired (increments are no longer the unit of work). Also locked: the live session
  checklist (`TaskCreate`/`TaskUpdate`) mirrors the plan's tasks so the owner can
  watch progress — explicitly a viewport, **not** a replacement for the PROGRESS.md
  write. `npx tsc --noEmit` added to the §5 gate list (it was already being run in
  practice but wasn't written down). Written into `execution-protocol.md` §2c–2d and
  §2.7/§5, summarised in `CLAUDE.md`.
- 2026-07-31: **Target system plan CLOSED and archived** (owner: "That plan is
  actually complete, you can mark it as so and archive it"). All 16 tasks done, every
  device check passed 2026-07-30/31. `target-system-plan.md` moved
  `Design/Plans/` → `Design/archive/` with a status banner noting it was executed
  (not superseded) and that its per-task-stop / ~400-line execution rules were retired
  the same day. Its relative links still resolve — same folder depth. `Design/Plans/`
  now holds only `D15-two-lever-truing-independent.md` and `dope-first-plan.md`.
- 2026-07-26: **Wooded Zero Range CLOSED** — owner confirmed all five stages plus
  the D16 raw-zero work and the true-MOA face fix ("All of that checks out, can
  mark it off the lists"). Every Wooded Zero row moved from `AWAITING OWNER` to
  `DONE`. Session total: 47 test files / 578 tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean,
  `vite build` clean.
- 2026-07-26: **`D16-raw-zero-error.md` archived** to `Design/archive/` on owner
  instruction, after the offset model + off-paper visibility shipped. NOTE: one
  consequence is still unbuilt — the DOPE book's "not zeroed" / "not chronoed"
  status notes need the Data Book UI (2.4f). Archiving the DECISION doc does not
  close that; it is carried in Deferred observations and should be picked up with
  2.4f. `Design/Plans/` now holds only `D15-two-lever-truing-independent.md` and
  `dope-first-plan.md`.
- 2026-07-26: **MOA target face resized to a TRUE-MOA grid** (owner, on device:
  "both appear fine at MIL but at MOA they're off. Not by a lot but definitely
  off"). Root cause was NOT the new range — it affected the original Zero Range
  identically and had since 2.3c. The MOA face was 22 in over a 22-cell grid,
  i.e. 1 inch per square, which relies on the shooter shorthand "1 inch = 1 MOA
  at 100 yd". The true figure is 1.0472 in, so every square was **4.5% small**,
  and the error ACCUMULATES — by the reticle's 10 MOA mark the grid had drifted
  ~0.45 MOA, exactly the widening mismatch in the screenshot. MIL was unaffected
  because 1 mil at 100 m is 10 cm BY DEFINITION, so its 2 cm squares are exactly
  0.2 mil. Face is now DERIVED (`22 x yardsToMeters(100) x moaToRad(1)` =
  0.58517 m = 23.04 in) rather than authored, in BOTH bays. Real "true MOA"
  targets are printed with 1.047 in squares for this same reason, so the new size
  is more physically correct, not a fudge. 4 tests in
  `wooded-zero-config.test.ts` pin one square = exactly 1.00 MOA / 0.20 mil and
  explicitly reject the old 22 in face.
- 2026-07-26: **D16 raw zero error implemented** (owner: "read the plan for
  D16-raw-zero and lets get that implemented"). Offset model changed from two
  independent ~1 MOA normal draws to a POLAR draw — magnitude uniform 5-35 MOA,
  direction uniform over the circle — decomposed to H/V. **Checked before
  building, per the owner's "check to be sure":** the offset change alone is
  small and contained, but it interacts with the new Wooded Zero Range. At the
  25 m station a 35 MOA error is 25.5 cm off centre, versus a 22 cm half-width on
  the metric paper — so **~16% of metric rifles would have fired at the
  "get on paper" station and left nothing on screen**, with no way to walk the
  correction in. Owner chose to **paint misses on the backer board** (the option
  D16 itself implies: "a backer big enough to catch a ~9″ first-shot miss"). The
  board is 33 cm half-width, so every draw now lands somewhere visible.
- 2026-07-26: **Three plan docs archived** (owner: "archive the range plan and also
  the 2.4a and 2.4e plans"). `mil-zero-range-plan.md`, `2.4a-computed-dope-data-model.md`
  and `2.4e-chronograph.md` moved `Design/Plans/` -> `Design/archive/`, with all 25
  references (source comments, `feature-catalog.md`, this file) repointed and the

  on-device check (canopy sway at the ambient breeze, framerate with shadows +

- 2026-07-26: **Wooded Zero Range approved (owner)** — plan
  `Design/archive/mil-zero-range-plan.md`, built in 5 stages. Decisions taken during
  planning: shooter on a **natural knoll** (elevation is ballistically free);
  **10.5 deg** target fan (wider was tested and buys nothing — corridor
  termination, not lateral separation, is what frees ground for trees);
  `unitCharacter: 'both'` (round yards **or** round metres, world built metric);
  **lane markers in**; **additive** range, Zero Range is NOT retired; **no berms
  anywhere** (board contrast comes from light + a dark tree mass behind each
  station); **shared** environment module so other ranges can adopt the upgrades;
